Strategist responds to ScoutComplete
View challenge

Strategist Response to Scout

Their position: Scout, citing pretix quotas, and Craftsman's draft invariants count capacity as confirmed + reserved + held seats (at most 10). Both treat reservations and short-lived holds as seat states that the transfer, swap and assign handlers must all count, and that the invariant and fuzz suite must cover.

My response: Modify

Reasoning: The council is arguing about two different things under one word. A reservation is mandatory: spec s.8 counts "active reservations" toward 10 even before arrival, s.10 says preferred no-show seats stay reserved with "no automatic expiry" until manual release, and acceptance scenario 1 tests exactly that. So reserved is a real state, and my round 1 Must #7 already counts it. Scout and Craftsman are right that it has to be in the invariant and fuzz suite. That means reserved-to-confirmed at check-in, release by an actor only, transfer and swap of a reserved guest, and a substitute taking over a reservation without a double claim. A short-lived hold is different. The spec marks it optional twice ("Temporary holds, if implemented", "Optional short-lived seat holds may reduce conflicts"), and it stays Won't. A synchronous check-then-db.save() handler leaves no window to protect. Delegation already gives each table one owner, so the only real contention is the Super Administrator against an owner, or one staffer on two devices. A hold would also make the arrival peak worse: a pick parked while a staffer talks to a guest blocks the last seat for everyone else. Holds would cost about 1.5 to 2 days (TTL, an expiry sweep, a fake clock in tests, release when a device walks away, ghost holds after a crash) to fix conflicts that a fast refusal with a reason code (SEAT_TAKEN, then refresh) already handles. Craftsman's real worry is that "handlers will forget to count them". I answer that with structure, not more states. Every seat claim is one record on the guest, seat: {tableId, seatNo, status: 'reserved'|'confirmed'}. One function, claimsAt(tableId), counts every active claim whatever its status, and every handler (assign, transfer, swap, substitute, reclassify, table removal) must call it. Then no handler can forget a status, because none of them counts by status. The invariant becomes claimsAt(t) <= capacity for every table. The fuzz suite drives random sequences of assign, check-in, release, transfer, swap and substitute over seeded reserved and confirmed guests (about half a day more than Craftsman's draft). It also asserts that the status set is closed, so a held state cannot creep in untested. If a later client app truly needs holds, they become one more claim status with an expiresAt. The status-agnostic count covers them on day one, so leaving holds out now costs nothing later. Net: reserved is In, with full invariant and fuzz coverage. held is Out, and the claim model and closed-enum test leave room to add it later.

Round 3: Converge

ArchitectComplete
View position

Architect Final Position — CUSTOM APP module and the YEP 2026 Registration & Table Assignment engine

Revised recommendation: Build CUSTOM APP (customapp) as a thin module shell whose first app is a generic event engine. Event data lives in CORE's existing db.json as ca* collections, and that is the one authoritative copy. Every state change goes through one synchronous commit(): it re-reads the actor's event role, checks revisions, checks invariants through one status-agnostic claimsAt(tableId), applies the change, saves, and undoes the change in memory if the save fails. Three cheap rules give the event its own recovery unit without splitting the file:

  1. replaceData() keeps the current ca* collections, so a CORE restore never touches an event.
  2. A versioned, event-scoped export is the single format for the laptop pull, the printed pack and any later import.
  3. Failed public attempts never write to disk.

Live sync is a short poll of changes?since=<seq> with a server-supplied pollMs. The contract has a wait parameter, so a long-poll can be switched on later without a client change.

I move storage back into db.json, because Operator and Strategist both showed that a split is more risk than a fix 12 days out. I keep everything that makes the system correct.

1. Where Round 2 left us, and how I resolve it

In Round 2, Operator and I each crossed over. I conceded a per-event store (data/customapp/<eventId>/). Operator conceded the shared db.json, with conditions. Strategist then cut the event-only import, the on-box snapshots and the long-poll. This is my resolution:

QuestionRound 1Round 2 (me)FinalWhy
Where event data livesdb.jsonPer-event state.json + log.jsonldb.json (ca* collections)A split changes the offsite allowlist (server/offsite.js:265), the cron, the restore check and RESTORE.md, none of it drilled, in 12 days. Operator is right that an undrilled recovery path is the worse risk at 3 AM.
The danger that drove my Round 2 flip: Admin "Restore a backup" erases check-insnot seenSolved by moving outSolved in place by a carve-out in replaceData()server/api.js:419-431 already keeps devlog, feedback, guideEdits and the helpdesk mail setup across a restore. Event data joins that list. It is one line, it follows the existing precedent, and a test covers it.
save() cost grows with COREMeasureSplitMeasure on 28 Nov, then use levers in orderOperator's ladder: compact JSON, then coalesce outbox status writes, then resize. Split only if p95 is still above about 150 ms, decided by 30 Nov.
Event restore endpointnonePOST .../restoreCut before 9 DecRecovery means corrections through normal commands, or the standby (a whole-file restore on a separate box rolls back nobody's work). The export format is fixed now, so a v1.1 import is a reader, not a redesign.
Live sync25 s long-polllong-pollShort poll, pollMs from the server, wait param reservedAvoids the requestTimeout and monitor-exclusion work. My Round 1 fallback was already "the same URL with wait=0", so this is the same contract at one speed.
HoldsOutOutOut. reserved In, counted by claimsAt()I agree with Strategist and Scout (see section 3).

2. Concessions made

  1. Storage location (second reversal, made deliberately). I withdraw the per-event state.json / log.jsonl store. My Round 2 reasons were:
  2. (a) a CORE restore erases check-ins;
  3. (b) save cost tracks CORE's size;
  4. (c) PII ends up in whole-CORE backups;
  5. (d) an event-scoped purge.

Each has a cheaper answer inside db.json:

  • (a) the replaceData() carve-out;
  • (b) measure, then compact JSON, coalescing and a resize;
  • (c) and (d) a post-event purge command removes ca* records for the event. Backups age out on the existing 30-day and offsite lifecycle. Guardian sets the retention date, and the dated nightly copies are documented as the residual.

The bounded context is kept in the code (only server/customapp/ touches ca*), not in the file layout.

  1. Long-poll becomes short poll. The server returns pollMs: 2000 for staff boards and 4000 for guest status pages, with a ceiling raised under load. An unchanged answer is {seq, unchanged:true}, a few bytes, computed without building the board. Long-poll stays a Could, behind the wait parameter.
  2. The event restore endpoint and on-box snapshots move to v1.1. I keep Operator's O6 (export JSON to data/backups/events/ every 5 minutes) as an option. It is XS once the export exists, and Operator and Strategist should settle it. My non-negotiable is the format, not the timer.
  3. Holds stay Won't. The naming follows Strategist: seat.status ∈ {reserved, confirmed}, counted by claimsAt(). That replaces my Round 1 seat.state and Scout's claimsOn.
  4. Public verification follows the merged Skeptic and Advocate spec, not my Round 1 single {ok:false, see:'desk'}. The data model changes this causes are in section 5.

3. Non-negotiables

  1. One writer, one synchronous commit(). There is no await between validation and db.save(), and a test enforces that commit.js contains no await. The process stays single: no queue mode, no cluster, no worker threads that write. n8n's Bull/Redis queue mode is rejected for this reason.
  2. One authoritative copy. There is no dual-write, no mirror file and no browser-side capacity check. Snapshots and exports are derived copies, with a seq and a sha256.
  3. Capacity is counted, never stored. claimsAt(tableId) counts every guest whose seat.tableId === tableId, whatever the status. Every handler that places or moves a seat calls the shared checkInvariants(post-state), which calls claimsAt. A closed-enum test fails if SEAT_STATUSES gains a value without a decision on how it counts.
  4. The seat lives inside the guest record, and the owner, the Super Administrator and the seat are single fields. This makes double seats, double delegation and two Super Administrators impossible to represent.
  5. A CORE restore never changes event state. replaceData() keeps caEvents, caTables, caGuests, caChanges and caOutbox from the live data, and a test asserts that every event's seq is unchanged after /api/admin/import. While any event is live, the Admin restore screen says "Event data is not part of a CORE restore".
  6. Signed-out traffic cannot cause a disk write, except a successful verification. Failed attempts, caps and referral codes live in bounded in-memory maps (section 5). Without this rule, an attacker on the QR page drives a whole-CORE JSON.stringify per request.
  7. Event endpoints never fall into the generic per-IP buckets (api-anon 120/min and public-write 20/h at server/api.js:558-562, 1445-1447). The event limiter is keyed per event and per normalised surname. Otherwise the venue NAT stops check-in after about the 20th guest.
  8. Exactly one Super Administrator per event, changed only through setSuperAdmin. That needs customapp.manage, password re-entry and an audit record.

4. Implementation notes

4.1 Module layout (unchanged from Round 1 apart from feed.js)
server/customapp/index.js            registerCustomAppRoutes(route, db, {audit, notify, mail})
server/customapp/events/model.js     accessors, normalisers (surname canon, last-4), lookup keys
server/customapp/events/commit.js    the unit of work (no await)
server/customapp/events/commands.js  plan() per command, pure
server/customapp/events/changes.js   seq, since-queries, scoped board builder (replaces feed.js waiters)
server/customapp/events/public.js    verify, status, referral map, caps (the only signed-out code)
server/customapp/events/outbox.js    capacity-1 FIFO drain (n8n ConcurrencyQueue shape), retries, emailPaused
server/customapp/events/io.js        import preview/apply with collision report, export (CSV, JSON v1, plan PDF)
shared/customapp.js                  APPS registry, enums (SEAT_STATUSES, ARRIVAL, APPROVAL), validators
shared/seating.js                    eligible(), claimsAt(), recommend(): pure, browser and server
public/event.html + public/js/event.js    guest page; server.js page list + security.js pagePolicy hash
4.2 commit() (final shape)
export function commit(ctx, eventId, user, cmd) {            // synchronous; a test greps for 'await'
  const ev = mustEvent(ctx.db, eventId);
  if (ev.flags.readOnly) throw conflict('read_only');          // see note on flags
  const role = roleOf(ev, user);                               // re-read every call: permission changes apply at once
  const prior = ctx.idem.get(cmd.requestId);                   // Map rebuilt from caChanges at boot
  if (prior) return prior.result;
  const plan = COMMANDS[cmd.type].plan(ctx.db, ev, role, cmd);  // pure; throws Conflict(code, fresh)
  expectRevs(plan, cmd.expect);                                // 409 stale
  checkInvariants(ctx.db, ev, plan);                           // I1-I10 on post-state, via claimsAt()
  const undo = apply(ctx.db, ev, plan);                        // rev++, ev.seq++, push compact caChanges entry
  try { ctx.db.save(); } catch { undo(); throw new HttpError(503, 'Not saved. Try again.'); }
  afterCommit(ctx, ev, plan);                                  // audit.record, outbox.enqueue (sync); no awaits
  return plan.result;                                          // {seq, revs, ...}
}
  • Flags (Operator O5) are fields on ev.flags: registrationOpen (public verify), emailPaused (outbox drain) and pollMs (poll response). I would keep readOnly as well. It costs one line above, and it is the switch the Super Administrator uses during a reconcile. Strategist kept it; Operator cut it. It is cheap enough that I side with keeping it.
  • Invariant check on demand: GET .../invariants runs the same checkInvariants over the whole event. Craftsman's fuzz oracle, the load-test post-check and the 60-second self-check (if Strategist keeps it) all call this one function.
4.3 Data model deltas from Round 1 Appendix B
ChangeFieldReason
Rename`guest.seat = {tableId, seatNo\null, status:'reserved'\'confirmed', at, by}`Converged naming. null means no claim. A release writes history, not a third status
Replaceguest.statusTokens: [{hash, issuedAt}] (at most 5; the oldest is dropped)One token per device (Skeptic). Staff revoke a token by removing its entry
Addguest.flags: [{kind:'reverified_other_device', at}]Advocate and Skeptic: detection without scolding. Shown in the owner's queue and the Super Administrator's alerts
Addguest.lookup.altSurnames[]Stored aliases (for example "dela Cruz" / "Cruz"), matched exactly after canonicalisation
Addevent.window = {opensAt, closesAt}Server-enforced activation window. Before it opens, an event-level "opens at" page accepts no input
Addevent.qrKeyHash (optional, P0/P1 for Strategist)Advocate's zero-typing presence key in the QR URL. One field and one constant-time compare. The Super Administrator rotates it. A missing or old key gets the uniform desk reply
CompactcaChanges entry = {seq, at, actorId, via, type, requestId, guestIds, tableIds, diff}Field-level diffs, not full before/after images. Estimate: about 3,000 entries × about 200 B ≈ 0.6 MB at the end of the event. This keeps db.json growth small
Unchangedtables, owners, Super Administrator, approval, substitution, outbox, invariants I1-I10
4.4 Public verification (merged spec, as architecture)
  • POST /api/public/event/:code/verify {surname, last4, k?}:
  • Check the event is live, the window is open and k matches (when enabled). Then canonicalise: NFKD, strip diacritics, lowercase, drop spaces, hyphens, periods and apostrophes, strip suffixes.
  • Look the guest up in guestsByLookupSurname (a lazy map, rebuilt when seq changes).
  • With exactly one match, the result is commit(selfCheckIn) (idempotent). It mints a token and adds the flag if the device is new. The reply is {token}.
  • Any failure gives one byte-identical reply with the same status code, plus R-xxx drawn from one random pool. The server pads both paths to a fixed minimum response time.
  • The referral map is held in memory: Map<code, {eventId, surnameCanon, reason, at}>, with a 3-hour TTL and at most 5,000 entries. The typed last 4 digits are never stored. Staff open GET /api/customapp/events/:id/referral/:code (signed in) to see the reason and a pre-filled search. A restart loses the codes, and staff then search by name. That is acceptable.
  • The surname cap is also held in memory: Map<eventId:surnameCanon, Set<distinct wrong last-4 hashes>>. Advocate counts distinct values, so a typo repeated counts once. On the threshold, I defer to Skeptic and Advocate. Skeptic proposes 8 per 15 minutes and Advocate 5 distinct per 10 minutes, and both sit inside the same structure. It clears on timeout, or when a staff member taps "Clear and check in". A global failure rate only raises an alarm to the Super Administrator (a notification); it never blocks.
  • Event limiter: createRateLimiter buckets keyed event:code (a global ceiling, for example 600/min) and event:code:surname. /api/public/event/ is exempt from api-anon and public-write. Status polls are keyed by token hash.
  • GET /api/public/event/:code/status?t=<token>&since=<seq> returns {seq, pollMs, state, firstName, table, seat}, or {seq, unchanged:true, pollMs}. The token goes in a query parameter, never in the path, so it stays out of the page URL history. Caddy and app logs must strip it. Operator, please check the access log format. The headers are Cache-Control: no-store and Referrer-Policy: no-referrer.
4.5 Live sync (short poll)
  • GET /api/customapp/events/:id/changes?since=<seq>&tab=<id> returns {seq, pollMs, unchanged:true} when seq is unchanged. That is one integer comparison, without building the board. If seq has moved, it returns {seq, pollMs, board}, scoped per the Round 1 access policy, with gzip from Caddy.
  • Budget: 30 staff at 2 s, plus about 60 pending guest pages at 4 s, is about 30 requests per second at peak. Unchanged replies cost microseconds. A changed board costs one scoped build per device per commit. Tuner checks this in load test 1 (4 Dec).
  • When the server's seq is behind since (the standby case), the client gets a full board.
  • Optimistic UI is not allowed. The client re-renders from the 409 fresh payload, or from the next poll.
4.6 Email (decision moves to 3 Dec)
  • caOutbox stays in db.json, and the drain runs outside commit(). Status writes go through a 2-second debounced db.save() (Operator's coalescing lever). The trade-off is that a crash can lose a status. Messages left in sending are re-queued after 2 minutes, which risks a rare duplicate email rather than a lost one.
  • Every async path in outbox.js is wrapped in try/catch, and it reports to the monitor. This sits alongside Operator's O3 process-level unhandledRejection logger, which lands on 28 Nov before any of this code.
  • The transport comes from smtpOptions() / canEmail(), extracted from server/helpdesk.js:245-261 into a shared mailTransport(db). If the 3 Dec go/no-go says "display + verbal", the outbox ships with emailPaused=true and the model is unchanged.
4.7 Export format (the one artifact for recovery)

GET /api/customapp/events/:id/export.json returns {format:'customapp.event/1', exportedAt, eventId, seq, sha256, event, tables, guests (without token hashes), changes, outbox}.

The same bytes feed:

  • the laptop pull every 5 minutes (Strategist and Operator);
  • the printed pack (CSV and PDF are derived from it);
  • the standby top-up reference;
  • the post-event archive handed to the client before the purge.

A v1.1 import reads this format and nothing else. Paper reconciliation stays as commands, sent with via:'reconcile' and occurredAt through commit(), so a paper overbooking is refused and listed rather than applied.

4.8 Migration and CORE touch points (all small)
  1. server/db.js: add ca* defaults to emptyData() / openDb. Switch to compact JSON (JSON.stringify(data)) only if the 28 Nov p95 is above 50 ms. Readers are unaffected, and the restore check parses either form.
  2. server/api.js replaceData(): keep the live ca* collections (one block next to guideEdits). Also exclude ca* guest PII from GET /api/admin/export, unless an "include events" option is ticked. Guardian decides the default; my proposal is to exclude while an event is live, because the full-backup download is otherwise a second copy of the guest list on someone's laptop.
  3. server/api.js limiter exemption for /api/public/event/. server.js and server/security.js get the event.html page and its CSP hash.
  4. shared/modules.js: customapp entry, and MODULE_API ^/api/(customapp|public/event)(/|$). shared/roles.js: eventstaff (customapp.use), plus customapp.manage.
  5. Guides: a new docs/guides page for Event staff and for the Super Administrator, plus whats-new and the screenshots, as the repository rules require.
4.9 Tests I depend on (owned by Craftsman)
  • A grep guard: commit.js contains no await.
  • Race tests: Promise.all of N handle() calls for the last seat. Exactly one succeeds, and the others get 409 full or seat_taken with fresh.
  • A fuzz suite over assign, check-in, release, transfer, swap, substitute, reclassify, table deactivate and delegate, seeded with reserved and confirmed guests. After every step, checkInvariants passes, holds are 0, and no reservation is released without an actor.
  • A closed-enum test on SEAT_STATUSES.
  • Restore isolation: after /api/admin/import, every event's seq and caGuests are unchanged.
  • Public no-write: 1,000 failed verifies cause 0 calls to db.save() (spy), and byte-identical bodies.
  • Save-failure undo: when save() is stubbed to throw, the state equals the pre-state and the reply is 503.
  • A journey test: two browser contexts race for the last seat. Then guest verify, the referral code, and "clear and check in" at the desk.
4.10 n8n patterns, final disposition
PatternFinal
Form Trigger responseMode: onReceivedBorrow. Verify answers at once with a token, and the status page polls
Form auth token bound to node, webhook and executionBorrow the binding. Random, hashed, per-device token bound to (event, guest). No JWT and no signing key
ConcurrencyQueue (capacity counter + FIFO)Borrow for the email outbox only. Seats fail fast with a 409 and never queue
Capacity reservation reserve() / release()The model if holds are ever added (an explicit release, never two per guest). Not built now
Push abstraction, SSE handshakeDefer. If it is added later, copy the connectionKey-replaces-older and heartbeat semantics. Push carries only {seq}, and data comes from the authorised GET
Queue mode, multi-mainReject. It breaks single-writer atomicity
4.11 What I need from others to close
  • Operator: the 28 Nov db.json size and save() p95. Whether logs strip the t= query parameter. Whether to keep the on-box export timer (O6).
  • Strategist: P0 or P1 for qrKeyHash. Whether readOnly stays (I recommend yes, at XS).
  • Skeptic and Advocate: one threshold for the surname cap (the structure supports either).
  • Guardian: the post-event purge date, and the admin-export default for event PII.
AdvocateComplete
View position

Advocate Final Position: YEP 2026 Registration & Table Assignment as CORE "CUSTOM APP"

Revised recommendation: Keep the three surfaces from Round 1, which are a tiny standalone guest page, a phone-first "My queue" for Event staff, and an "Event control" screen for the Super Administrator. They now sit on the council's converged mechanics:

  • one synchronous commit() per event;
  • one status-agnostic claimsAt(table);
  • reserved and confirmed claims only, with no holds;
  • short polling paced by a server-supplied pollMs;
  • kill-switch flags;
  • a dashboard in its simple form.

Every one of those mechanics has to show up to people in one of two ways. A guest sees a calm sentence that sends them to the desk with a code. A staffer sees a recovery screen that already holds fresh options. Neither ever sees a bare error, a lock screen or a 429. The earlier launch blocker still stands: the current anonymous limits (server/api.js:558-562, applied at 1445/1447: 120 GETs/min and 20 writes/hour per IP) would refuse real guests who share the venue's NAT address. Event routes therefore need their own buckets, keyed per event and per surname, and the single-IP load test has to show zero 429s to real guests.

Concessions made:

  1. No holds. The meter shows two states. Scout and Strategist are right. A single-request staff commit has no checkout window to protect, and a hold that a staffer leaves parked while talking to a guest would block the last seat at the arrival peak. In Round 1 the 10-dot meter had "ring = reserved/held". It now reads filled = confirmed (seated) and ring = reserved (preferred, waiting). The tile's text label repeats the counts ("6 seated · 2 reserved · 2 free"), so colour and shape are never the only cue. A lost last seat is the 409 seat_taken recovery I specified, re-ranked in place.
  2. Skeptic's cap numbers, counted my way. The per-surname soft cap is 8 distinct wrong last-4 values per (event, normalised surname) in 15 minutes. It clears itself after 15 minutes, and any staffer can clear it in one tap from the referral record. Retyping the same wrong value counts once. A capped surname gets the same uniform desk reply as every other failure.
  3. Exact matching on the normalised form, with no fuzzy matching on the public page. Lenient canonicalisation still applies: NFKD, strip diacritics, lowercase, drop spaces, hyphens, periods and apostrophes, strip Jr./Sr./III, and match altSurnames[]. Edit distance, partial surnames and first-name fallback stay behind staff sign-in, in assisted search.
  4. Pre-event collision report (Skeptic's condition), built as an onboarding screen. It appears as the "Import check" step in Setup, described in the implementation notes.
  5. No presence key and no desk code in the Must list. The activation window, enforced on the server, is the real control against remote spoofing. I still oppose a typed desk code that every guest has to enter. If Strategist finds an XS slot after the 5 Dec load test, my preference is a zero-typing key in the QR URL (/event/yep26?k=…) that the Super Administrator can rotate. Otherwise both stay Won't for 9 Dec.
  6. Short polling, not long-poll or SSE. The status page and queue use pollMs from the poll response (about 4 s while a guest waits, 10 s once seated, staff 3 s), pause on visibilitychange, and back off while offline. "Feels live" means a status that is honest about how old it is ("Updated 6:41 PM", "Last updated 2 s ago"). It does not need push.
  7. Event control is built in its simple form. I accept Strategist's fallback dashboard: a counts row, an occupancy grid and the event-health strip. My per-staffer workload panel becomes a plain sortable table (staffer · waiting · longest wait · last active). The "Redistribute pending…" wizard becomes a per-guest or per-table "Move to…" action. Bulk redistribution is a follow-up.
  8. Email can fall back to "display + verbal" on 3 Dec. If email is cut, the status page and the printed A–Z list carry the table number. The guest page then drops the "email sent" line and adds "Screenshot this screen". The seat is never blocked or delayed by email in either case.
  9. No QR redirect for failover, and no persisted sessions. During a failover, guests go to staff-assisted check-in. What I need in exchange is cheap, and it is listed under implementation notes: easel copy, a desk card with the standby URL, and staff told that a restart signs them out.
  10. Where the event data lives. Architect wants a per-event store and Operator wants the shared db.json with per-event import. This is not my call. My one user-facing requirement is that no recovery action may erase check-ins people have already seen. A guest told "Table 5" must not be told something else after a restore. Whichever option wins, replaying the journal has to come before staff resume.

Non-negotiables:

  1. No guest-facing dead end. Every public failure (no match, ambiguous, capped, malformed, unlisted, released reservation) returns identical copy, layout and status code, plus a random referral code: "Please see the registration desk and show this code: R-482." The guest never sees "not found", "invalid", "already checked in", a countdown or a lock screen. The staff side sees the real reason.
  2. Zero 429s to legitimate guests from one shared IP. Event routes get their own limiter keyed per event and per surname, with a high per-IP ceiling sized for one NAT address. A high global failure rate alerts the Super Administrator and never blocks. This passes or fails in the 5–6 Dec single-IP load test.
  3. Check-in is separate from seating. Any event staffer can check a guest in at the desk. Only the owner or the Super Administrator can seat them. Each is its own transition and its own endpoint.
  4. Every staff rejection is a recovery screen. A 409 carries a stable code (seat_taken, table_full, not_owner, guest_moved, stale, no_eligible_seat), a human sentence, who or what won where that can be shared, the current guest state, and fresh ranked options. With all of that in the one reply, the sheet re-renders in place with no second round trip. A guest moved by the Super Administrator greys out with "Moved to Maria's queue by the Super Administrator, 6:52 PM" before it leaves. Nothing vanishes silently.
  5. Re-verifying returns the same status, with no scolding. It creates no second check-in and only adds a "verified again from another device" flag for staff. Each verify mints a per-device token (32 bytes, stored SHA-256, revocable), so polling never resends surname or last 4.
  6. Two taps to seat a guest on a 390 px phone. Tap the card, tap the table (seat chip pre-selected), then Confirm. The Confirm label repeats the choice ("Seat at Table 5"). Tables outside the staffer's scope are visible and read-only, labelled "Not yours".
  7. The Super Administrator's exceptions inbox is Must, even in the simple dashboard. It holds approvals for walk-ins and substitutes, "No seat in scope", released reservations of late arrivals, and capped surnames. The spec gives the Super Administrator these decisions, so without an inbox they turn into phone calls at peak.
  8. Accessibility floor on every surface:
  9. inputs at least 16 px and targets at least 48 px;
  10. WCAG AA contrast in both CORE themes;
  11. table number at 56 px or more;
  12. aria-live="polite" on the status region, with focus moved to the result heading;
  13. everything works by keyboard;
  14. class badges and meter states carry text, not colour alone;
  15. prefers-reduced-motion is honoured.

Implementation notes:

Guest page /event/<code> (standalone HTML like support.html, CSP-hashed in server/security.js pagePolicy, noindex, Cache-Control: no-store, target under 40 KB, no web fonts)

  • States: closed ("Registration opens at 6:00 PM", no inputs) · verify · submitting · seated (preferred: "Welcome, Ms. Dela Cruz. Table 3, Seat 4") · pending ("You're checked in. We're choosing your table. This page updates by itself.") · referred (uniform code) · offline (last known status kept, "Will update when you reconnect"). The selfCheckIn=false and readOnly flags render as the referred-style desk sentence, not as an error.
  • Fields: Surname (placeholder "e.g. Dela Cruz") and "Last 4 digits of your mobile" (inputmode="numeric", pattern="\d{4}", autocomplete="off"). A "Use email instead" link swaps the second field to the full email address, because the last 4 characters of an email mean nothing.
  • On success, history.replaceState moves the page to /event/<code>/s/<token>, as support.js already does. After 4 minutes in pending the page adds "Still choosing. A staff member can help at the desk", which matches the staff card turning amber at 4 minutes.
  • Timing parity: the server pads failure replies to the success path's timing. Tuner confirms this under load.

Staff "My queue" (landing page for the Event staff account type; this is a SPA view under the Custom App module)

  • Sticky bar: event name, staffer name, "Last updated 2 s ago". If updates stop for more than 15 s, the bar turns amber and reads "Reconnecting…".
  • Sections: Waiting for table (n), oldest first · Checked in, seated · Find guest.
  • Card: name, company, industry chip, class badge in text, and a wait timer (amber at 4 min, red at 8 min).
  • Bottom sheet: up to 3 recommendations, each with a reason line ("Same industry: Banking, 3 of 6 seated") and a meter, then "Other eligible tables" (collapsed), then read-only "Not yours". Seat chips run 1–10, taken seats are disabled and the first free seat is pre-selected. Seat numbers are mandatory for preferred guests.
  • Group seating: select mode uses checkboxes (not long-press), then "Seat together (n)". Only tables with n or more free seats are ranked. Splitting a group needs an explicit "Split group" confirm. A group whose members belong to different owners shows "Send group to Super Administrator".
  • Empty queue: "No one is waiting for you. Your tables: 5, 6, 7 (14 seats left)."

Find guest / assisted check-in

  • Search by partial name, company or referral code. Entering R-482 pre-fills what the guest typed and shows the real reason.
  • Results show name, company, status and contact masked as "•••• 4417".
  • Actions: Check in (any staffer), Seat (owner only), Clear cap and check in (one tap), Add walk-in, Record substitute. The last two go to the Super Administrator's inbox. A substitute is shown as one seat changing hands ("Seat 3-4: Juan Reyes replaces Pedro Reyes").

Event control (Super Administrator), simple form

  • Counts row: Invited · Checked in · Not yet arrived · Waiting for table · Needs approval · Seats left.
  • Occupancy grid of table tiles: number, class label, 10-dot meter plus a text count, owner. Full tables are dimmed and labelled "Full".
  • Health strip, owned by Operator: seq, save p95, outbox depth, 429 count, failure-rate alarm.
  • Exceptions inbox and workload table. Flags are toggles with confirm dialogs that name the effect, for example "Turn off self check-in? Guests will be sent to the desk."
  • Releasing a reservation needs a confirm that names the guest, table and seat. Removing a table with guests is disabled and gives the reason, with a "Move guests…" action.

Setup → Import check (collision report as onboarding)

  • After import, one screen lists each kind of problem as a count with a fix action next to it:
  • ambiguous pairs (same normalised surname and same last 4): add the full email, add a direct mobile, or mark "desk only";
  • guests with no mobile and no email: mark "desk only";
  • possible duplicates;
  • guests without an industry.
  • "Open registration" stays disabled with the reason "3 issues to resolve" until the list is clear or the Super Administrator acknowledges it. This makes Skeptic's condition a guided step instead of a document.

Failover and offline (the UX side of the cuts)

  • Easel card under the QR: "If this page doesn't open, please go to the registration desk."
  • Staff desk card: the standby URL, "If the app restarts you'll be signed out. Sign in again and you'll land back on your queue", and the paper procedure.
  • Sign-in returns the user to the route they were on. The session policy (server/auth.js:54, 8 h idle and 24 h max) covers a sign-in at 4 PM, so no change to auth.js is needed.
  • The print pack (A–Z list, per-table sheets, per-staffer sheets with pre-partitioned free seats) is stamped "as of <time>" and fed by the laptop export pull. After recovery, "Reconcile paper seating" replays each entry through the normal commands and shows any capacity conflict as a recovery card.

Copy rules: address guests by title and surname. Write sentences, not codes. Staff copy names the person and the time. Every guest-facing dead end points to a human.

Tests I need from Craftsman, as Playwright journeys at 390 px:

  • two contexts race for the last seat, and the loser sees "Table 5 just filled. Updated options:" with the guest still selected;
  • one journey per row of the merged verification table: closed, match preferred, match pending → seated, uniform failure, capped → cleared at the desk in one action, re-verify from a second device;
  • the not_owner / guest_moved greyed card;
  • "Import check" blocks opening registration until resolved.

Guides (CORE convention, required by test/guides.test.js):

  • A new docs/guides/custom-app-events.md for the Event staff account type and admins, with the sections "Seat a guest", "When a table fills up", "Find a guest at the desk", "Event control (Super Administrator)" and "If the system is down".
  • A whats-new.md line and screenshots in screenshots.json.
  • This guide also serves as the spec's operator guide, and the one-page desk card is printed from it.
SkepticComplete
View position

Skeptic Final Position: CUSTOM APP / YEP 2026 Registration and Table Assignment

Lens: red. Pre-mortem, attack surface, Swiss cheese, Chesterton's fence. Re-checked for this round: server/api.js (limiter buckets at 556-562, signin-fail keyed by client.ip at 648-688, signInFailuresPerAddress is a security setting, not a constant), server.js and server/*.js (no unhandledRejection or uncaughtException handler anywhere). n8n caveat: the n8n repository is not in this workspace. Any n8n reference here is from model knowledge and is marked (unverified).

Revised recommendation

The council has converged on mechanics I can sign: one synchronous commit() per event, a status-agnostic claimsAt(table), reserved and confirmed claims with no holds, short polling, kill-switch flags checked inside commit(), and a soft, desk-forgiving guest verification. I no longer see a seating-logic risk that should block the plan.

The risks that remain are all in CORE's existing safeguards, which were built for an office ERP and not for a ballroom on one NAT address:

  • per-IP limits on guest pages and staff sign-in;
  • the CORE admin * bypass;
  • a process that dies on the first unhandled promise rejection;
  • an Admin "Restore a backup" that can silently erase check-ins;
  • a deploy pipeline that restarts production on any push.

Each of these must be closed in code or in config, then proven by the single-IP load test and the 6 Dec chaos drill. A runbook promise does not count. If those five are closed, I accept the rest of the risk register as tolerable for 9 December.

Concessions made

  1. Holds are out (Scout, Strategist). I had already cut them in Round 1, and the council's

argument is now stronger than mine: a single-request staff commit has no checkout window to protect, and a parked hold would block the last seat at peak. A lost last seat is a 409 seat_taken with fresh options. reserved stays, with no expiry, released only by hand (spec s.8, s.10).

  1. No hard lockouts (Advocate). A per-surname lockout lets one person block "Dela Cruz" at the

door. It becomes a soft cap: 8 distinct wrong last-4 values per (event, normalised surname) in 15 minutes. Retyping the same wrong value counts once. The cap clears itself after 15 minutes or in one tap at the desk. There is no per-event global lockout; a high failure rate alerts the Super Administrator and never blocks.

  1. Re-verify shows the seat (Advocate). I withdraw "a second self-check-in does not re-show

the seat". A spoofer learns only a table number on the first try anyway, and hiding it punishes the real guest on a second phone. Re-verify is idempotent: same status, no scolding, no second check-in, and a "verified again from another device" flag for staff.

  1. Lenient normalisation (Advocate). The surname is not the secret; the last 4 and the

activation window are. NFKD, strip diacritics, lowercase, drop spaces, hyphens, periods and apostrophes, strip Jr./Sr./III, match altSurnames[]. My condition stands: matching stays exact on the normalised form.

  1. Referral codes (Advocate). Accepted, because they are compatible with my uniform-reply rule

if every failure is byte-identical apart from the random code.

  1. No desk code or presence key for 9 Dec. The server-enforced activation window is the real

control against remote pre-arrival spoofing. Both stay Won't unless Strategist finds an XS slot after the 5 Dec load test, and then Advocate's zero-typing rotatable QR key is preferred over a typed desk code.

  1. No SSE, no long-poll. Short polling paced by a server-supplied pollMs is the lowest-risk

live sync: no long-lived connections, no reconnect storm after a restart, no session-touch questions.

  1. No persisted staff sessions. Changing VAPT-hardened auth.js 12 days out is a bigger risk

than the one it removes. I accept "restart signs every staff device out", but only with the technical freeze, the crash handler and the venue sign-in allowance below. A freeze without them means one unhandled rejection at 18:40 signs out all staff, and then the per-IP sign-in limit locks them out.

  1. Storage location: not my call, but one condition (Architect vs Operator). Both options are

acceptable to me. What I will not accept is any path where a CORE-wide restore can silently overwrite live event data:

  • Per-event store (Architect): this closes the db.replace hazard structurally, because a

full CORE restore does not touch data/customapp/. The cost is offsite, cron and restore-check changes, which must be drilled on 6 Dec.

  • Shared db.json (Operator): this is the smaller change, but it needs a code guard, not

a runbook line. The Admin restore (api.js:1314) must refuse while any event window is open, unless a CORE admin re-enters their password and types the event code to confirm. It must be audited, and a test must prove that a CORE restore cannot change an event's seq without that confirmation. Whichever wins, the event-only restore must be rehearsed end to end before 7 Dec. If it has not been rehearsed, it does not exist.

  1. Ops scope trimmed (Operator, Strategist). Event-only import as a separate feature, on-box

snapshot tooling beyond the 5-minute copies, warm standby and persisted sessions are deferred. The cheap items (freeze guard, kept prior release, crash logger, flags in commit(), invariant check, save() measurement on 28 Nov, laptop export pulls) cover the failure modes I care about.

Non-negotiables

These must be in place before the event is activated. Each has a test or a drill that proves it.

#SafeguardProof
N1Event authority is event data. event.superAdminUserId (exactly one) plus delegations, checked by eventCan(user, event, action, target). CORE admin * grants no guest or table writes. A CORE admin's only event power is an audited "transfer Super Administrator" (2-step, reason, old holder revoked in the same commit).Unit test: a CORE admin's seat write returns 403; a transfer leaves exactly one Super Administrator.
N2One mutation path. Every seat, owner or status change goes through the synchronous commit(): re-read the user and delegations, check expectedRev and clientOpId, check flags, build the candidate state, check invariants I1 to I6 (Round 1 Appendix C), apply, persist. No await between check and write. If the persist step throws, undo in memory and return 503, never a false success.Lint-style test that no handler mutates event state outside commit(); save-failure test.
N3Capacity counts every claim. A single claimsAt(table) counts every status that occupies a seat, every handler calls it, and a test fails when a new status is added without being classified.Status-exhaustiveness test plus the fuzz suite (random ops, invariants after each step, zero holds, no expiry release).
N4Idempotency and staleness. clientOpId replay returns the first result (kept 24 h per event). Per-guest and per-table rev; a mismatch returns 409 with a stable code and fresh options. This also applies to Super Administrator actions.Interleaved handle() tests for all 12 acceptance scenarios, including double-tap and last seat.
N5No per-IP limits on legitimate guests or staff at the venue. Event public routes leave the global public-write/api-anon buckets and get their own limiter, keyed per event, per surname (failures) and per status token (polls), with a high per-IP ceiling sized for one NAT address. Staff sign-in: raise signInFailuresPerAddress (a setting, so no code change) or allow the venue's known egress IP for the event window, audited.Single-IP load test, 4-5 Dec: 300 guest pollers plus 15 staff, with a "restart at peak, all staff re-sign in" step. Zero 429s to real guests and staff.
N6Uniform guest failure reply. No match, ambiguous, capped, malformed, unlisted and window-closed-mid-request all return the same status code, copy and layout, with timing padded to the success path, plus a random referral code from one pool. The real reason is visible to staff only.Test comparing response bodies minus the code; timing parity measured under load.
N7Activation window enforced on the server. Before doors-open the page shows "Registration opens at 6:00 PM" and accepts no input; verify routes refuse. The Super Administrator can open it early or close it, audited.Unit test on the window boundaries (stored as UTC instants, shown in Asia/Manila).
N8Status token. 32 bytes random, stored as SHA-256, per device, scoped to one guest, revocable by staff, persisted with the event (survives a restart). Responses carry Cache-Control: no-store and only title or first name, table, seat and state. Polling never resends surname or last 4.Test: poll with an unknown token returns the uniform reply; poll after restart still works.
N9Pre-event collision report cleared before activation. It lists every pair with the same normalised surname and last 4, every guest with no mobile and no email, and import problems (duplicates, unknown classification or industry, capacity over 10). Activation is refused until it is clear or acknowledged by the Super Administrator.Preflight test with a seeded collision.
N10The process does not die on a stray promise. Add process.on('unhandledRejection') and uncaughtException loggers (none exist today, and Node 22 exits on an unhandled rejection by default). The rejection handler logs and keeps serving; the exception handler logs and exits so systemd restarts cleanly.Chaos drill: inject a rejected promise in a non-event route during the load test; the event keeps serving.
N11Technical deploy freeze, 8 Dec 18:00 to 10 Dec 09:00. A DEPLOY_FREEZE file checked by deploy/aws/update.sh and the environment: production reviewer gate. A kept prior release enables rollback in under 60 s. Event-day fixes use kill-switch flags, not code.Push a test commit on 8 Dec after 18:00 on purpose; it must not restart production.
N12No silent loss of check-ins on restore (see concession 9).Test plus the 6 Dec drill.
N13Module switch cannot be turned off mid-event by accident. Turning CUSTOM APP off in Admin panel → Modules while an event window is open needs Super Administrator confirmation, or is locked.Unit test on the modules route.

Implementation notes

Guest public route (/event/<code>, /api/public/event/:code/*)

  • Accept only application/json, with a body cap of about 1 KB. No multipart, no file fields, and

no echo of submitted fields in the reply. This is the lesson from content-type confusion in public form and webhook handlers (n8n advisory, unverified).

  • Validate input: surname of 1 to 80 characters after normalisation, with zero-width and control

characters stripped; last 4 must match ^\d{4}$; email is the full address, lowercased and trimmed, max 254 characters. Anything else gets the uniform reply, not a 400 with detail.

  • Last-4 comparison uses the digits-only form of the stored mobile ("+63 917 123 4567",

"09171234567" and "917-123-4567" all compare on "4567").

  • Order of checks: limiter, then window, then match. Never return early before the limiter; pad

failures to match success timing.

  • The soft-cap counter is a Map keyed by eventId|normSurname, holding a set of distinct wrong

values with timestamps. In-memory is acceptable: a restart clears the caps, and that fails toward the guest. A global failure-rate alarm (for example over 30 failures in 5 minutes) goes to the Super Administrator's exceptions inbox.

  • The status poll handler is O(1): token hash, then guest, then a small projection. It must not

re-serialise the event or touch any session. Honour pollMs (about 4 s while waiting, 10 s once seated), with jitter.

  • CSP hash in server/security.js pagePolicy, noindex, no-store, and the same pattern as

support.html.

Staff routes (/api/customapp/*)

  • customapp.use only opens the module. Every write calls eventCan on every guest and every

destination table in the plan. Any out-of-scope element rejects the whole plan (group and cross-scope transfers).

  • Re-read delegations from the event state at commit time and never cache them, so a reallocation

takes effect on the old owner's next write (403) and next poll (UI refresh).

  • Out-of-scope rows in the read-only availability view show name, company, industry and table

only. No contact fields. Exports are Super Administrator only.

  • Required reasons: override, release, cross-scope move, Super Administrator transfer, and opening

the window early.

Data hygiene

  • On import and edit, strip CR/LF and control characters from names and companies (email header

injection). Use composeMessage encoded words; never put a raw name in a header.

  • Size cap of about 2 MB and 2,000 rows on import, dry-run diff before apply, Super Administrator

only. Once the event is live, re-import is merge-by-external-ref and never touches status, seat or owner fields.

  • On CSV export, prefix cells starting with = + - @, tab or CR with ' (check shared/csv.js).
  • Audit labels use the guest ref and a masked name, never mobile or email, so the hash-chained log

holds no purgeable PII. Event snapshots and laptop copies hold PII and join the retention timer that starts 10 Dec.

  • Assignment email uses a separate events From or Reply-To, so guest replies do not land in the

helpdesk IMAP mailbox as IT tickets. If email is cut on 3 Dec, the status page and the printed A-Z list carry the table number.

Kill-switch flags (checked inside commit() and the public routes)

  • At minimum: selfCheckIn (off sends guests to the desk with the uniform reply), readOnly

(refuses all commits; used during restore), and emailSend. Flag changes are Super Administrator only and audited.

Tests and drills Skeptic will check before signing off

  1. All 12 spec acceptance scenarios as interleaved node --test cases against handle().
  2. Fuzz: random sequences of check-in, seat, release, transfer, swap, substitute and reallocate,

with invariants asserted after every step and a status-exhaustiveness check.

  1. Playwright multi-context journey: two staff, same last seat, one 409 with fresh options.
  2. 4-5 Dec single-IP load test on a production-sized copy (not production): zero 429s,

state.json or save() p95 within Operator's thresholds, restart at peak with all staff re-signing in under 3 minutes.

  1. 6 Dec chaos drill: kill -9 at peak, injected unhandled rejection, event-only restore to a chosen

seq, CORE Admin restore attempted while the window is open (must be refused or must leave the event untouched), Super Administrator transfer in under 2 minutes.

  1. 8 Dec: deliberate push after the freeze starts, which must not deploy.

Residual risks accepted

  • Pre-arrival spoof by someone who knows a colleague's direct mobile. Bounded by the activation

window, the usher's "show this screen" step and the re-verify flag.

  • A restart signs every staff device out. Bounded by the freeze, the crash handler, the venue

sign-in allowance and a rehearsed re-login.

  • One Super Administrator is a single point of failure (spec requirement). Bounded by the rehearsed

CORE-admin transfer.

  • In-memory surname caps reset on restart. This fails toward the guest, which is the safe

direction.

StrategistComplete
View position

Strategist Final Position — CUSTOM APP and the YEP 2026 Registration & Table Assignment app

Lens: gold. Value per unit of effort, the MVP cut line, sequencing against a fixed date. Inputs: my Round 1 position, my Round 2 responses to Operator and Scout, the Round 2 challenges (Operator, Advocate, Skeptic, Architect, Scout), and the Round 3 finals of Architect and Advocate. Checked in the repo for this round:

  • server/api.js:416-431: replaceData() already keeps devlog, feedback, guideEdits, the

helpdesk mail setup, and the CRM and Projects setup across a restore. Architect's carve-out follows this precedent.

  • A grep of server.js and server/ finds no unhandledRejection handler.

n8n: not cloned locally. n8n references come from peers' citations or model knowledge, and the latter are marked (unverified).

Revised recommendation: Keep the three tiers. Each tier ends with CORE event-ready, so a slip costs a feature and never the event:

  • Tier 0, staff-only seating: released dark by 1 Dec.
  • Tier 1, guest QR page plus delegated queues: by 3 Dec.
  • Tier 2, exceptions, email and dashboard: by 5 Dec, with the client acceptance demo.

No feature work after 6 Dec. The council has now converged on the build:

  • event data stays in db.json as ca* collections, which a CORE restore never touches;
  • one synchronous commit() per change, counting seats through a status-agnostic claimsAt();
  • reserved is in and held is out;
  • short polling with a pollMs set by the server;
  • a hardened guest page that never writes to disk on a failed attempt;
  • about one developer-day of cheap operations safeguards, front-loaded on 28 Nov.

The new items are cheap and are paid for by the cuts. The build stays at about 15 dev-days, with 6 to 8 Dec as buffer.

Concessions made:

  1. Restore safety moves into Tier 0. In Round 1 I missed that Admin "Restore a backup"

(db.replace) would wipe event state. I add Architect's replaceData() carve-out (XS, plus a test that every event's seq is unchanged after a restore) as a Must. It is cheaper than Architect's Round 2 per-event store, which I opposed, and it closes the same hole.

  1. The event export becomes a fixed, versioned format (JSON v1) in Tier 0. It used to be a

Tier 2 by-product of CSV export. One format feeds the laptop pull, the printed pack, the standby and a v1.1 import. Fixing it now makes the deferred import a reader, not a redesign.

  1. On-box 5-minute export copies are back in, at XS. In Round 2 I cut "on-box snapshots" as an

S. Once the export exists, writing it to data/backups/events/ every 5 minutes is a timer. Operator and Architect both want it. With 300 guests the file is small, so it does not slow the event loop.

  1. Operations item #24 grows from XS to S, and its dates move earlier, as Operator asked.
  2. On 28 Nov, about half a developer-day: the freeze guard in remote-deploy.sh, keeping

/opt/ltdc-erp-prev before the rsync --delete, and the unhandledRejection logger.

  • Staging is rebuilt from S3 between 1 and 3 Dec. That is the restore drill, and staging then

serves as the load-test target and the warm standby. This replaces my 4 Dec date.

  • A first load test runs on 4 Dec against the Tier 1 build, and a second on 6 Dec with the

kill -9 drill.

  • All of this is operator time except the 28 Nov half-day.
  • The storage decision has a gate, not an assumption. Measure db.json size and save() p95

on 28 Nov, on a copy of production. If it is above 50 ms, apply in order: compact JSON, then coalesced outbox writes, then a resize (check the Elastic IP first). Split the store only if p95 is still above 150 ms, decided by 30 Nov. Above that line the split costs about 2 dev-days, and email is dropped to "display + verbal" to pay for it.

  1. **The guest page is hardened as Skeptic and Advocate jointly specified, adding about 0.5

dev-day to Tier 1:**

  • every failure gets the same reply, carrying a referral code;
  • name normalisation is deterministic, with exact matching only (no fuzzy matching in

public);

  • a hashed random status token is kept per device, so polling never resends the surname or

last 4;

  • an activation window;
  • re-verifying is idempotent: the guest sees the same status, and staff see a flag;
  • a soft cap per surname of 8 distinct wrong last-4 values in 15 minutes, which staff can

clear in one tap or which times out;

  • a high failure rate alerts the Super Administrator and never locks the event;
  • failed attempts live only in bounded in-memory maps.

I also add the pre-event name-collision report as a Must, folded into the import preview at no extra item. For remote spoofing I take Advocate's zero-typing presence key in the QR URL (XS). A typed rotating desk code is a Could, off by default: it costs every guest a step to stop an unlikely attack.

  1. Fuzz coverage of reserved (+0.5 day). Random sequences of assign, check-in, release,

transfer, swap, substitute and group arrival are run over seeded reserved and confirmed guests. The suite asserts zero holds, no release by expiry, and a closed SEAT_STATUSES set.

Non-negotiables:

  1. One synchronous commit() for every change. No await between validation and save(),

enforced by a test. The actor's event role is re-read on every call. The change is undone in memory if the save fails. There is one process and no queue mode. This is the leverage that makes transfer, swap, groups and substitutes size S. Without it, the Must list does not fit in 12 days.

  1. Capacity is counted, never stored. claimsAt(tableId) counts every seat claim whatever its

status, and every handler calls it. reserved is Must, because spec sections 8, 10 and 21 and the no-show scenario require it. held is Won't. A closed-enum test forces a decision before any status is added.

  1. A CORE restore never changes event state, through the replaceData() carve-out and its

test. This ships in Tier 0, before any real guest data is imported.

  1. Event endpoints never fall into the generic per-IP buckets (api-anon 120/min and

public-write 20/h). Otherwise the venue's shared NAT stops check-in at about the 20th guest. Signed-out traffic causes a disk write only on a successful verification.

  1. One Super Administrator per event, held as a field on the event and not as CORE's *.

Changed only through an audited setSuperAdmin.

  1. The core loop is above the cut line: verified guest, then an eligible table chosen, then

capacity holds, then the guest sees the table. That covers backlog #2, #5 to #7, #12 to #14 and #21. The fallback order may never touch them.

  1. Dates:
  2. client decisions (section 22) by 1 Dec;
  3. final guest list, industries and table inventory by 3 Dec;
  4. email go/no-go on 3 Dec;
  5. data freeze and last feature work on 6 Dec;
  6. code freeze on 7 Dec at 18:00;
  7. deploy freeze from 8 Dec 12:00 to 10 Dec 09:00.
  8. Still Won't before 9 Dec:
  9. SSE or WebSocket push;
  10. seat holds;
  11. an event-only import endpoint (format fixed, reader in v1.1);
  12. persisted sessions (they would change the VAPT-hardened auth.js);
  13. long-poll (the wait parameter is reserved);
  14. a QR redirect host;
  15. the floor plan, SMS and analytics;
  16. any generic app framework or CORE-wide outbox.

Implementation notes: see below.

3. Fallback order if we slip (unchanged in spirit, extended)

Apply these one at a time, at the first missed tier date:

  1. Email becomes display plus verbal. Spec section 6 allows this, and the "email failure"

scenario must work without email anyway.

  1. The dashboard becomes counts plus the occupancy list.
  2. Groups become one-by-one assignment with a capacity hint.
  3. The seat picker for non-preferred guests is dropped. Seats stay mandatory for preferred

guests.

  1. The presence key is dropped, and the activation window plus last 4 remain the controls.

Never dropped:

  • commit() and claimsAt();
  • the replaceData() carve-out;
  • event-scoped rate-limit buckets;
  • the guest page with uniform replies;
  • delegation and queues;
  • the acceptance and fuzz suites;
  • the printed contingency pack.

4. Reusable without over-engineering (final line)

"Reusable" means parameterised:

  • every record is keyed by eventId;
  • classifications, table types, industries and relationships are data;
  • no YEP strings appear in code;
  • the module shell has an APPS registry with one entry.

It does not mean abstracted: no app builder, no custom fields, no plugin loading, no generic outbox. The bounded context lives in code: only server/customapp/ touches ca*. It does not live in the file layout. The cost is about 5% of the build. Event #2 reuses the engine as it is. Anything more is decided after 9 Dec, from the metrics below.

5. n8n patterns: what we borrow and what we reject

  • Borrowed (pattern, not code):
  • Form Trigger: a hosted public form with a fixed response mode becomes the guest page with one

uniform reply (unverified).

  • The concurrency-control queue becomes the capacity-1 FIFO email outbox, kept outside

commit(), with retry and the emailPaused flag (per Architect's citation).

  • Credentials kept server-side become reuse of the helpdesk SMTP setup; no new secrets store.
  • Rejected:
  • Queue mode with Bull/Redis workers: it breaks single-thread atomicity.
  • The push connection (SSE/WS): it is new infrastructure in server.js that a 2 to 4 second

poll makes unnecessary for about 15 staff and 300 status pages.

  • Execution locking across processes: there is only one writer.

6. Success metrics

Hard gates, checked by the invariant scan after each load test and after the event:

  • zero overbooked tables;
  • zero duplicate check-ins;
  • zero double seats;
  • zero holds;
  • zero reservations released by expiry;
  • exactly one Super Administrator.

Event-day flow:

  • QR scan to "checked in": median under 30 s.
  • Checked in to seated (regular guests and employees): p90 under 2 minutes at peak.
  • Share self-verified versus assisted, which decides whether to invest in the guest page for

event #2.

  • Share seated at a recommended table, which says whether the ranking helps.
  • Email delivered, failed and resent.

Operations:

  • At least 4 production deploys exercise the freeze guard and rollback before 7 Dec, including

one deliberate rollback.

  • save() p95 under 100 ms at the load profile.
  • Zero 429s to genuine guests from one IP.
  • In the kill -9 drill, staff are back in or on paper within 3 minutes.
  • Paper reconciliation through normal commands gives zero invariant violations.
ScoutComplete
View position

Scout Final Position — CUSTOM APP / YEP 2026 Registration & Table Assignment

Revised recommendation: Build the write path on the pretix check-in contract and the guest path on n8n's waiting-form pattern, and add no new infrastructure.

The pretix contract has four parts:

  • Every write carries a client opId (pretix's nonce).
  • A refusal returns a 409 with a machine reason and the current seq.
  • One status-agnostic claimsAt(table) counter covers both reserved and confirmed.
  • The synchronous commit() takes the role of pretix's lock-before-availability-check.

The n8n waiting-form pattern has three parts:

  • The verify POST answers at once, as in onReceived.
  • It mints an opaque status token, stored hashed.
  • The status page polls a status endpoint. Outside the activation window, every input gets the same neutral reply, as an inactive production webhook does.

For live sync, I move from long-poll to short poll: a server-supplied pollMs plus a ?since=seq delta. SSE moves to Hold for 9 Dec. n8n's concurrency queue is used only to throttle the email outbox; seat requests fail fast and never wait in a queue.

One finding comes before any public build: today's per-IP limits would lock the guest crowd and the staff out on the venue network. The event therefore needs its own rate-limit buckets and a single-source-IP load test.

Concessions made
#What I gave upRound 1 positionWhy I moved
1Short-lived holds (held)pretix-style capacity = confirmed + reserved + heldStrategist and I agreed this in Round 2. pretix cart positions and Seats.io holdTokens exist to protect a buyer's checkout window, which spans several requests. A staff assignment is one request, and so is a group's all-or-none assignment, so there is no window to protect. A TTL hold would also add a write that no request triggers (an expiry sweeper), which would need its own audit trail and restart recovery. It would also block the last seat at the arrival peak. Seat states are {reserved, confirmed} and a closed-enum test guards the set.
2Long-poll as the Adopt-ring transportLong-poll on since was Adopt and SSE was TrialStrategist's short poll with a server-supplied pollMs takes away the 120 s held-request timeout, the Caddy buffering question and the exclusion from the request-duration monitor. It is also the prior-art pattern itself: n8n's waiting-form page polls its status suffix (packages/cli/src/webhooks/waiting-forms.ts:58,252, WAITING_FORMS_EXECUTION_STATUS), and CORE's badges already poll. The latency cost is about pollMs/2 on average. That is fine for a "waiting for your table" page and for staff queues at 2-3 s. SSE (sse.push.ts shape) moves to Hold until after 9 Dec. The seq cursor stays, so SSE can be added later without a redesign.
3An event-wide brake on verification failures"an event-wide brake on failures" as a rate-limit bucketAdvocate is right that a global cap is a denial-of-service lever: one script could stop every guest at 6:45 PM. The evidence I cited supports controls on what can be searched (Cvent OnArrival restricts kiosk search fields), not a global lockout. A high event-wide failure rate now alarms the Super Administrator. Only the per-surname soft cap blocks: it counts distinct wrong last-4 values, routes the guest to the desk with the uniform reply, and staff can clear it in one tap. Advocate proposed 5 per 10 min and Skeptic 8 per 15 min. Both are within the range of prior art, so I defer the exact number to them.
4pretix-style offline devicesPresented pretixSCAN's upload-later model with force + datetime as the offline precedentWe will not build device-side offline caching in 12 days. The precedent I cited (FooEvents, Ticketsolve) warns that two offline devices can accept the same guest. What I keep is pretix's reconciliation contract. The paper fallback is replayed through the normal commit() with the original timestamp and an opId, so re-entering a paper sheet twice is a no-op and capacity and duplicate checks still run. Architect's "replay commands through commit()" is that same contract.
5HMAC-signed URLs and IP allowlistsAlready Skip/Reject in Round 1No change. I list them here so that nobody adds them back.
Storage location: where I stand on the crossed concessions

Architect and Operator each conceded to the other in Round 2. Architect now wants data/customapp/<eventId>/, and Operator now accepts the shared db.json. No prior art settles this: pretix keeps every event in one database and restores at database level, and n8n keeps workflows and executions in one database. So this is a CORE-specific call, and I defer it to Architect, Operator and Strategist. Here is what I checked in the code, so the decision rests on facts:

  • Operator is right that the offsite allowlist is hard-coded. server/offsite.js:265 rejects any manifest file other than db.json, audit-head.json and audit-log.jsonl. The nightly restore check (deploy/aws/ltdc-erp-restore-check.service → scripts/restore-check.js) knows only that set.
  • Architect is right that the change is bounded. offsite.js:280-290 already restores a documents/ directory from a separate manifest.documents map with name checks and sha256. An event directory would follow that precedent.
  • Architect's new hazard is real. POST /api/admin/import (server/api.js:1315) calls replaceData → db.replace (server/db.js:118), which swaps the whole of db.data. If the event lives in db.json, a CORE restore during the event erases check-ins.
  • Save cost. db.save() writes JSON.stringify(data, null, 2) (db.js:125), so pretty-printing inflates every commit. Compact JSON is a one-line saving whichever option wins.

My tie-breaker is the "boring technology" rule: for 9 Dec, prefer the path that the nightly restore check already exercises. That is the shared db.json, provided the restore hazard is closed cheaply. Either replaceData carries over the live customApp subtree, or /api/admin/import refuses while any event window is open. A test must assert that a CORE restore leaves every event's seq unchanged. If the 28 Nov save() measurement goes past Operator's 150 ms line, Architect's directory store is the right fallback, built on the documents/ precedent.

Non-negotiables
  1. Event-specific rate limits, tested from a single IP address. Today the limits in server/api.js:1447-1448 work like this:
  2. api-anon allows 120 requests per minute per IP, on every anonymous call.
  3. public-write allows 20 per hour per IP, on every non-GET /api/public/*.
  4. auth allows 20 sign-ins per minute per IP (api.js:557).

Behind venue-Wi-Fi NAT or carrier CGNAT, the 21st guest in an hour gets "Too many messages from this network". About 8 status-polling phones use up api-anon, and staff signing in together hit auth. The fix:

  • /api/public/event/* is exempt from public-write and api-anon.
  • It gets its own buckets: per status token (polls), per IP with a crowd-sized ceiling while the window is open, and per surname (the soft cap above).
  • The auth limit is raised for the event window only.

The 4-5 Dec load test drives 300 guests and the staff from one source IP.

  1. opId idempotency on every event write: check-in, assign, release, transfer, swap, substitute, approve, group assign and paper replay. Storing (opId → result) per event and returning the stored result on a repeat is what keeps venue-Wi-Fi retries and paper re-entry from becoming double writes.
  2. Structured refusal reasons, as in pretix already_redeemed / ambiguous / blocked. Every staff-side refusal is 409 {reason, seq}, with reason ∈ {table_full, already_checked_in, out_of_scope, stale, not_eligible, not_open, read_only}, and the client refreshes. Guest-side failures are the opposite: one byte-identical reply with a random referral code, and the reason stored server-side for staff.
  3. One status-agnostic capacity counter. claimsAt(tableId) counts every active claim whatever its status. Every handler calls it, and an exhaustiveness test fails if a new seat status appears without a decision on whether it counts.
  4. No public enumeration. Public matching is exact on the normalised form: no fuzzy matching, no candidate list and no first-name fallback (the Cvent restricted-fields precedent). Fuzzy and partial search exist only in staff-assisted search behind sign-in.
  5. One process, synchronous commit, nothing awaited between the check and the save. No queue mode, Redis, cluster, or WebSocket dependency. n8n's own shouldRelayViaPubSub (packages/cli/src/push/index.ts) shows that leaving a single process brings a pub/sub relay with it.
  6. Seat requests are never queued. The FIFO from n8n concurrency-queue.ts applies to the outbox only. The spec wants the loser refreshed or escalated.
  7. A whole-CORE restore must never silently change event data. This holds whichever storage option wins, and a test must assert it.
Implementation notes

Tests that the prior art implies (for Craftsman)

  • Send the same opId twice. Expect one change and an identical response.
  • Two devices race for the last seat. Expect exactly one 200 and one 409 table_full with the current seq.
  • Replay a paper sheet twice. Expect it to apply once.
  • Every guest failure path (no match, ambiguous, capped, not open, unlisted) returns the same status, the same body shape and the same byte length apart from the code.
  • From one source IP, 300 verifies and 300 phones polling for 30 minutes get zero 429s on event paths.
  • A CORE restore through /api/admin/import leaves every event's seq unchanged.
  • SEAT_STATUSES is closed and held is absent.

Concessions made
  1. Storage stays in db.json, and I accept Architect's carve-out in place of my event-only import. In Round 2 Architect and I crossed over: he moved to a per-event store while I moved to the shared file. The Round 3 resolution is the replaceData() carve-out. It is one line, it follows the existing precedent for devlog, guideEdits, helpdesk, CRM and Projects, and a test checks it. That closes the "Admin restore erases check-ins" hole more cheaply than either my event import or his state.json and log.jsonl pair. I give up the event-only import and the restore endpoint before 9 Dec. Recovery means corrections through normal commands, or the standby. Why: a recovery tool built in a hurry, which overwrites live event data and is only ever used under pressure, is a bigger 3 AM risk than the gap it fills.
  2. Short poll, not long-poll. I drop the 25 s long-poll, its heartbeat and the exclusion from the Security monitor. The wait parameter is reserved. Why: this removes the server.requestTimeout (120 s) interaction completely, and pollMs gives me the same lever under load.
  3. Persisted sessions are not needed. A restart still signs every staffer out. I accept that, because the freeze removes deploy restarts and the logger removes the most likely crash. Skeptic's NAT-sized auth bucket and staff on the 4G hotspot make the re-sign-in survivable. Why: changing the VAPT-hardened auth.js 10 days out is the riskier move.
  4. No QR-redirect host, and standby is for staff only. If the primary is lost, guests are checked in by staff at the desk. Why: DNS and redirect machinery for one evening does not pay for itself.
  5. The health panel becomes a strip on dashboard #20, not a second screen. Process health comes from the existing Security monitor plus a free external 1-minute uptime check. Why: I would rather instrument what the Super Administrator will actually look at.
  6. The 60 s invariant self-check stays as Strategist placed it (Tier 1, XS). I had cut it to "on demand" in Round 2, and I withdraw that cut. It is the oracle for the load test and the reconciliation check.
  7. readOnly stays. I cut it in Round 2. Architect and Strategist both keep it at one line in commit(), and the Super Administrator needs it during a reconcile. I side with them.
  8. The trigger for splitting the store is latency, not size: above 50 ms, pull the levers; above 150 ms on 30 Nov, split. This is the gate that Architect and Strategist adopted. I hold to it and do not reopen it.

Implementation notes

A. 28 Nov ops half-day (code, about 0.5 dev-day, in this order)

# remote-deploy.sh, immediately after the "is it installed" check, before any unit install
if [ -f "$APP/data/DEPLOY_FREEZE" ] && [ -z "${DEPLOY_FREEZE_OVERRIDE:-}" ]; then
  echo "Deploys are frozen ($(cat "$APP/data/DEPLOY_FREEZE")). Nothing was changed."; exit 3
fi
# update.sh, before `systemctl stop`
rm -rf /opt/ltdc-erp-prev && rsync -a --exclude /data/ "$APP/" /opt/ltdc-erp-prev/
# /usr/local/sbin/ltdc-erp-rollback: stop; rsync -a --delete --exclude /data/ /opt/ltdc-erp-prev/ $APP/; same chown/chmod as update.sh; start; health loop
  • The workflow step that calls SSM needs an optional freeze_override input. Without it, the job fails red, so a blocked deploy is visible and not silent.
  • Exit code 3 must surface as a failed deploy job.
  • unhandledRejection handler: console.error with the stack, monitor.record('process','unhandledRejection'), no process.exit. Leave uncaughtException at Node's default (exit, then systemd restarts in 3 s), because the state is undefined after one.
  • Dry-run ltdc-erp-rollback on staging once it exists (1-3 Dec), and on production once in the 4 deploys before 7 Dec.

  • ls -l /opt/ltdc-erp/data/db.json.

  • Check free -m and the instance type.
  • Check for an Elastic IP (aws ec2 describe-addresses). If there is none, a resize stop/start changes the public IP. Then either allocate one first, which is a 5-minute DNS change done that day, or skip the resize.

F. Event-day runbook (goes into docs/guides with #22 and #23)

T-3h

  1. DEPLOY_FREEZE is present and the reviewer gate is on.
  2. Check the offsite drop-in with list-timers.
  3. Run a manual offsite backup, then confirm it in the Security monitor.
  4. Top up the standby from the newest copy.
  5. Smoke test on the rehearsal guest: verify, check in, seat, status, email to the sink.
  6. Newest export under 5 min old, and the laptop has pulled it.
  7. Print the pack, with every page marked "as of HH:MM".
  8. Check staff devices: signed in, on 4G, charged.

Doors

  • Set registrationOpen=true.
  • The on-call developer sits beside the Super Administrator with ltdc-erp-rollback, this runbook and the standby URL.
