  4. Set a retention date on the event.

10 Dec, 09:00

  1. Remove the freeze and the drop-in.
  2. Resize back.
  3. Take a final snapshot of staging, then decommission it.
  4. Start the PII purge clock (Guardian #33). It covers the ca* records in db.json, the 30 nightly copies, the event export copies and the offsite bucket lifecycle.

**G. n8n patterns, operations side *(unverified)***

  • Concurrency-limited execution: becomes the outbox drain, with at most N SMTP sends in flight, retry with backoff and a status per message.
  • Form Trigger "respond on received": check-in replies straight away, and email goes out later, outside commit().
  • Rejected: queue mode (Bull/Redis workers) and multi-main. A second process breaks single-thread atomicity and adds a Redis that can fail at 3 AM. This is unanimous across the council.

H. Cost

  • Resize to t3.small for about 3 days: about US$1.
  • Staging and standby for about 10 days: about US$3 to US$5.
  • Offsite every 15 minutes for one day: a negligible number of S3 PUTs.
  • External uptime check: free tier.
  • Total: under US$10.
CraftsmanComplete
View position

Craftsman Final Position: CUSTOM APP / YEP 2026 Registration & Table Assignment

Lens: purple. Testability, patterns, regression protection, and the developer who changes this code on 7 December with guests arriving in 48 hours.

Inputs: my Round 1 position; the Round 2 challenges (Operator x2, Strategist x2, Architect, Advocate, Skeptic, Scout); the Round 3 finals of Architect, Advocate, Scout, Skeptic and Strategist.

Re-checked in the repository for this round:

  • server/api.js:418-431: replaceData() keeps devlog, feedback, guideEdits with the

pattern next.x?.length ? next.x : db.data.x. That pattern prefers the backup's copy when it is non-empty. Copied verbatim for event data, it would bring back stale check-ins from the backup. The event carve-out must be "always keep live", and that needs its own test (see N3).

  • server/util.js:15-20: HttpError(status, message) still carries no structured payload.
  • scripts/journeys.mjs:43: journeys still spawn CORE with RATE_LIMITS: 'off'.

n8n: not cloned in this workspace. n8n references below are the ones Architect and Scout cited (waiting-forms.ts status polling, timingSafeEqual on the resume token, typed push messages) or are marked (unverified).

Revised recommendation

The council converged on mechanics that make my test strategy smaller and stronger, not bigger: one synchronous commit() per change, one status-agnostic claimsAt(tableId), claim statuses {reserved, confirmed} only, event data in db.json as ca* collections that a CORE restore never touches, short polling with a server-supplied pollMs, and kill-switch flags checked inside commit().

My recommendation is now:

  1. Keep the engine shape: a pure, synchronous plan(state, cmd, actor, now) inside commit().

It is what lets the hardest guarantees be tested in milliseconds with no database.

  1. Keep one shared checkInvariants(), now built on claimsAt(), run after every unit test,

after every fuzz step, at the end of the load script and by the 60-second production self-check (Strategist's item, the same function).

  1. Cut the suite to what each tier needs, about 120 focused tests instead of ~200, with the 12

spec acceptance scenarios as 12 named tests that are the client's evidence pack on 5 Dec.

  1. Add tests for the new council decisions, each one small and each one closing a hole that the

current suite would let through silently: the restore carve-out, the closed status enum, the kill switches, the undo-on-save-failure, the failed-attempt-never-writes rule, the single-IP guest load, the versioned export, and the unhandledRejection logger.

Concessions made

#What I relaxedRound 1FinalWhy
C1HoldsInvariant 1 counted confirmed + reservations + holds; fuzz generated holdsNo held state. Invariant 1 is claimsAt(t) <= capacity. Fuzz asserts zero holds and no expiry pathScout and Strategist are right: a single-request commit has no checkout window, and holds add the only write no request triggers (an expiry sweeper). My worry was "a handler forgets to count a status". Strategist's structural fix (count claims, never statuses) removes the bug class better than more tests would.
C2Long-poll sync testcustomapp-sync: "long-poll resolves on commit"Short-poll contract test: changes?since=seq returns only newer changes, carries pollMs, and ignores wait for nowShort polling removes the 120 s timeout and monitor-exclusion work. The test pins the contract so a later long-poll is a server change only.
C3Offline replay and pre-partitioned rangescustomapp-offline: replay with original timestamps, two-desk rangesDropped ranges. Paper allocations are re-entered through the normal commands, each with an opId; the test asserts a repeated opId is idempotent and a conflicting one is refusedNo device-side offline mode in 12 days (Scout C4). The event-only import is cut (Strategist), so there is no replay engine to test.
C4Event-only import / restore testsImplied by Operator's importNone before 9 Dec. Instead, a golden-file test for export JSON v1 so the v1.1 import is a reader of a frozen formatStrategist and Architect cut the import. Freezing the format now is the cheap part that keeps the later import safe.
C5Race test volumeReal-socket race x20 runs at freeze; 10 parallel staffRace test stays (4 cases), run x5 at freezeThe pure engine plus the mutation() guard carry most of the atomicity proof. The socket test is a smoke alarm, not the evidence.
C6Coverage numbersPer-file targets (95 / 85 / 75 %)Report only, no gate, no per-file targets in the planNo tool to add, no time to argue about percentages. Every refusal code having a test (checked by a catalogue test) is a better gate.
C7Second fixture event scopeWhole engine, rank and invariants suite re-run on "Test Gala"One customapp-reuse file: the acceptance-critical engine cases parametrised over two fixtures, plus a grep that server/customapp contains no YEP stringStrategist's "reuse by parametrising, not abstracting". Proves no hard-coding without doubling the suite.
C8The multi-device browser journey4 contexts, ~10 checks, Tier 0Same journey, but it lands with Tier 1 (3 Dec); Tier 0 relies on node testsThe guest page and queues do not exist in Tier 0; a journey written early would be rewritten.
C9Guest abuse rows follow the converged rulesHard per-token and per-IP capsSoft cap: 8 distinct wrong last-4 values per (event, normalised surname) per 15 min; same wrong value counts once; staff clear in one tap; no per-event global lockout (alarm only); re-verify idempotent with a staff flagAdvocate and Skeptic agreed the numbers; my job is to pin them in tests so nobody "tightens" them on 7 Dec.

Non-negotiables

N1. No await between check and save, enforced in code. Every event write registers through mutation(), which refuses an AsyncFunction at startup, and commit() throws in tests if a handler or plan() returns a thenable. A sync wrapper around a promise is the realistic 6 December regression; the AsyncFunction check alone does not catch it.

N2. One claimsAt(tableId), and a closed status set. SEAT_STATUSES is frozen in shared/customapp/. A test fails if any status is not classified, and a source test fails if any file other than engine.js reads seat.status to count capacity.

N3. A CORE restore never touches event data, and a test proves it. Restore a backup taken at seq 50 while live is at seq 120: every event's seq, guests and claims are unchanged. Also covered: "Erase all data" and demo data. This is the case where copying the existing devlog/guideEdits pattern would be wrong.

N4. checkInvariants() is one function used in four places: after every engine test, after every fuzz step (seeded, replayable, 200 seeds in CI and 1000 at freeze), at the end of the load script, and by the 60-second production self-check. A checker test breaks each rule by hand, so the checker cannot be vacuous.

N5. The 12 spec acceptance scenarios are 12 named node --test cases, green by their tier's date (table below). Test names quote the spec rows; the output is the 5 Dec evidence pack.

N6. Refusals are typed. HttpError(status, message, {code, fresh}) (backward compatible) and a frozen refusal catalogue shared with the browser. Tests assert code, never message text. A catalogue test fails if a code has no test that produces it.

N7. The guest crowd is tested on one IP with limits ON. An in-process test with TRUST_PROXY=1: 300 verifies plus status polling from one x-forwarded-for gives zero 429 on the event buckets, while the per-surname soft cap still trips. The browser journeys run with limits off, so without this test the #1 launch blocker is invisible to CI.

N8. A failed public attempt never writes to disk. Count db.save() calls around 50 failed verifies: zero. Every failure reply is byte-identical apart from the random referral code.

N9. Event files run alone in under 5 s: node --test test/customapp-*.test.js, with npm run test:event as the documented loop. If they are slow, they get skipped at the venue rehearsal.

N10. After the 6 Dec freeze, every fix lands with a test that failed before it. Same commit, same CI gate; the version bump, devlog entry and guide update ship with it.

Two shared-code changes, each with its own test file entry:

  • server/util.js: optional third HttpError argument; handle() serialises {error, code, fresh}.
  • server/api.js replaceData(): ca* collections always taken from db.data, never from next.

The guard, revised for N1:

// server/customapp/routes.js
export function mutation(route, method, path, perm, handler) {
  if (handler.constructor.name === 'AsyncFunction')
    throw new Error(`${method} ${path}: event writes must be synchronous`);
  route(method, path, perm, (req) => {
    const out = handler(req);
    if (out && typeof out.then === 'function')
      throw new Error(`${method} ${path}: event write returned a promise`);
    return out;
  });
}

Quality gates
  • Per edit: npm run test:event under 5 s (fuzz at 50 seeds).
  • Per push (existing workflow, unchanged): full npm test plus npm run journeys gate deploy.

Event files add about 10 s. The freeze guard Operator adds to remote-deploy.sh sits after this gate, so CI still proves every push even during the freeze.

  • Per tier: that tier's acceptance rows green; version bump, devlog entry, docs/guides

page for the Event staff and Super Administrator roles, whats-new line and retaken screenshots in the same commit (test/guides.test.js fails otherwise; add customapp to its MODULES list and update modules-roadmap / favorite-modules tests).

  • Freeze candidate (6 Dec): fuzz at 1000 seeds, race x5, load test 2 green, tagged commit.
  • Event night: the 60-second self-check runs checkInvariants() and puts a banner on Event

control if it ever returns a violation.

  • Review checklist for every event PR: no await in engine.js/commit.js; every write via

mutation(); no capacity count outside claimsAt(); refusals from the catalogue; new rules in checkInvariants(); tests assert codes, not text; no YEP string in server/customapp.

Effort

About 3 dev-days of test work in total, spread across tiers, not a separate phase: about 1.5 in Tier 0 (engine, invariants, fuzz with reservations, which includes the +0.5 day Strategist priced, api, restore, flags, export, race), about 1 in Tier 1 (public, scope, rank, sync, journey) and about 0.5 in Tier 2 plus the load script. Tests are written with each command, in the same commit, never "after the feature".

n8n patterns, through this lens
  • Waiting-form status contract (cited by Architect and Scout: waiting-forms.ts,

timingSafeEqual, status polling). Copy the contract into customapp-public rows: opaque token, uniform reply, a status endpoint polled at a server-chosen pace.

  • Typed push messages (Architect: abstract.push.ts). Even with polling, the change records

returned by changes?since use a small closed set of type values shared from shared/customapp/, so a later SSE transport reuses them.

  • Concurrency control as its own service (unverified detail): applied only to the email

outbox throttle, tested alone with fakeSmtp.

  • Not borrowed: queue mode, workers, multi-main locking. They would break the single-thread

atomicity the whole test strategy rests on.

Verdict

In progress

CUSTOM APP (customapp, a new entry in shared/modules.js) is a thin CORE module that holds client-specific apps behind an APPS registry. Its first app is a generic event-registration engine with events, guests, tables, delegation, check-in and seating, and Legati Group's YEP 2026 (9 Dec 2026, about 300 guests, a hard limit of 10 per table) is the first event loaded into it. Here "reusable" means parameterised, not abstracted. Every record is keyed by eventId. Classifications, table types, industries and their relationships are data, and the code contains no YEP strings. There is no app builder, no custom fields and no plugin loading. Event data lives in CORE's existing db.json as ca* collections, and only server/customapp/ reads or writes them. Every state change goes through one synchronous commit(). It checks the flags, re-reads the actor's event role, checks idempotency and revisions, plans the change, checks the invariants (capacity is counted by a single status-agnostic claimsAt(tableId)), applies the change, and calls db.save(). If the save throws, it undoes the change in memory. Because nothing is awaited between the check and the write, Node's single thread makes "last seat: only one succeeds", atomic swaps and transfers, and "a permission change applies at once" hold by construction. Seat claims have exactly two statuses, reserved and confirmed. Holds are out.

Live sync is a short poll on changes?since=<seq>, paced by a pollMs the server returns, so there is no new connection infrastructure. Guests use a hardened standalone page at /event/<code>. They type their surname and the last 4 digits of their mobile (or their full email). An exact match on the normalised surname checks them in and issues a hashed per-device status token. Every failure gets one byte-identical "please see the desk, code R-482" reply, and a failed attempt never writes to disk. Event routes have their own rate-limit buckets, sized for a ballroom behind one NAT address. The build ships in three tiers, and each tier leaves CORE ready for the event:

  • Tier 0, staff-only seating: 1 Dec, released dark.
  • Tier 1, guest QR page, delegated queues and recommendations: 3 Dec.
  • Tier 2, exceptions, email and dashboard: 5 Dec, with the client acceptance demo.

It is protected by a small operations layer that ships first, on 28 Nov: a freeze guard, a kept previous release with a one-command rollback, and an unhandledRejection logger. Then come two load tests from one source IP, a kill -9 drill, a technical deploy freeze from 8 Dec 12:00 to 10 Dec 09:00, a warm standby for staff, and a printed contingency pack. The build is about 15 dev-days, and 6 to 8 Dec are buffer.

Full design document

Design Document: YEP 2026 Registration & Table Assignment as the first app of the CORE module "CUSTOM APP"

Session: custom-app-yep-2026-registration-20261127-0748 · Date: 27 Nov 2026 (12 days to the event) · Scope: analysis and design only. Nothing is built until the user approves. Perspectives: Architect, Advocate, Skeptic, Strategist, Scout, Operator, Craftsman (three rounds). Spec: spec.txt (Legati Group, "Registration & table assignment", developer brief of 27 Nov 2026).

> Where the n8n references come from. In Round 1 Architect and Scout read a sparse clone of > n8n-io/n8n (Scout cites commit bafcff7) and quote file paths from it. That clone is not in this > workspace any more. The other perspectives marked their n8n references (unverified). Every n8n > item in this document is a pattern to copy, never code or a dependency. CORE stays on Node's > standard library.

Overview

CUSTOM APP (customapp, a new entry in shared/modules.js) is a thin CORE module that holds client-specific apps behind an APPS registry. Its first app is a generic event-registration engine with events, guests, tables, delegation, check-in and seating, and Legati Group's YEP 2026 (9 Dec 2026, about 300 guests, a hard limit of 10 per table) is the first event loaded into it. Here "reusable" means parameterised, not abstracted. Every record is keyed by eventId. Classifications, table types, industries and their relationships are data, and the code contains no YEP strings. There is no app builder, no custom fields and no plugin loading. Event data lives in CORE's existing db.json as ca* collections, and only server/customapp/ reads or writes them. Every state change goes through one synchronous commit(). It checks the flags, re-reads the actor's event role, checks idempotency and revisions, plans the change, checks the invariants (capacity is counted by a single status-agnostic claimsAt(tableId)), applies the change, and calls db.save(). If the save throws, it undoes the change in memory. Because nothing is awaited between the check and the write, Node's single thread makes "last seat: only one succeeds", atomic swaps and transfers, and "a permission change applies at once" hold by construction. Seat claims have exactly two statuses, reserved and confirmed. Holds are out.

Live sync is a short poll on changes?since=<seq>, paced by a pollMs the server returns, so there is no new connection infrastructure. Guests use a hardened standalone page at /event/<code>. They type their surname and the last 4 digits of their mobile (or their full email). An exact match on the normalised surname checks them in and issues a hashed per-device status token. Every failure gets one byte-identical "please see the desk, code R-482" reply, and a failed attempt never writes to disk. Event routes have their own rate-limit buckets, sized for a ballroom behind one NAT address. The build ships in three tiers, and each tier leaves CORE ready for the event:

  • Tier 0, staff-only seating: 1 Dec, released dark.
  • Tier 1, guest QR page, delegated queues and recommendations: 3 Dec.
  • Tier 2, exceptions, email and dashboard: 5 Dec, with the client acceptance demo.

It is protected by a small operations layer that ships first, on 28 Nov: a freeze guard, a kept previous release with a one-command rollback, and an unhandledRejection logger. Then come two load tests from one source IP, a kill -9 drill, a technical deploy freeze from 8 Dec 12:00 to 10 Dec 09:00, a warm standby for staff, and a printed contingency pack. The build is about 15 dev-days, and 6 to 8 Dec are buffer.

Architecture (Architect)

A1. Module boundary and file layout
shared/customapp/statuses.js      SEAT_STATUSES = freeze(['reserved','confirmed']), ARRIVAL, APPROVAL, FLAGS, CHANGE_TYPES
shared/customapp/refusals.js      frozen refusal catalogue (A6), shared with the browser
shared/customapp/seating.js       eligible(), claimsAt(), recommend(): pure; the server is authoritative, the browser uses them for display only
shared/customapp/invariants.js    checkInvariants(eventState) -> [] | [{rule, ids, detail}]
server/customapp/index.js         registerCustomAppRoutes(route, db, {audit, notify, mail}); APPS registry (one entry: 'events')
server/customapp/events/model.js  accessors, normalisers (surname canon, digits-only last 4), lookup keys
server/customapp/events/engine.js plan(state, cmd, actor, now) per command: pure, sync, no I/O, no Date.now()
server/customapp/events/commit.js the unit of work (no await; a test enforces this)
server/customapp/events/routes.js mutation() guard plus the staff routes
server/customapp/events/changes.js seq, since-queries, scoped board builder
server/customapp/events/public.js verify, status token, activation window, referral map, soft cap (the only signed-out code)
server/customapp/events/outbox.js capacity-1 FIFO email drain, retries, emailPaused
server/customapp/events/io.js     import preview/apply plus collision report; export (CSV, JSON v1, printable plan)
public/event.html + public/js/event.js        standalone guest page (support.html precedent)
public/js/views/customapp*.js                  My queue, Find guest, Event control, Setup

Changes to CORE outside the module are deliberately small:

  1. server/db.js: ca* defaults in emptyData() and openDb. Switch to compact JSON only if the

28 Nov gate asks for it (A8).

  1. server/api.js replaceData() gets the event carve-out (A7). /api/public/event/ is exempt

from api-anon and public-write and gets event buckets (C2). /api/admin/export leaves out event PII while an event is live, unless "include events" is ticked (audited).

  1. server/util.js: HttpError(status, message, {code, fresh}), where the third argument is

optional and backward compatible. handle() serialises {error, code, fresh}.

  1. shared/modules.js gets a customapp entry, MODULE_API

^/api/(customapp|public/event)(/|$) and MODULE_PARTS. shared/roles.js gets an eventstaff account type (customapp.use only) and the customapp.manage permission.

  1. server.js and server/security.js get the event.html page route and its CSP hash in

pagePolicy.

  1. server/helpdesk.js:245-261: smtpOptions() and canEmail() are extracted into a shared

mailTransport(db).

  1. A process-level unhandledRejection logger (Operator O3).
A2. Data model (db.json, ca* collections)
CollectionKey fieldsNotes
caEvents`id, app:'events', code, name, venue, date, status: draft\live\closed\archived, superAdminUserId, staff:[{userId, role, active}], industries:[{id, name, related[], active}], settings:{hardLimit:10, seatNumbers: preferred\all\none, mixedTables, releaseDelegable, emailFrom, replyTo, template}, window:{opensAt, closesAt}, flags:{registrationOpen, emailPaused, readOnly, pollMs}, qrKeyHash?, seq, counters, rev`code is a slug with a random suffix (yep26-k3f9), so the page cannot be guessed before the QR is shown. Exactly one Super Administrator by construction (a single field). related is kept symmetric on write. Window times are UTC instants, shown in Asia/Manila.
caTables`id, eventId, number, classification: preferred\regular\employee\mixed, capacity (1..hardLimit), ownerUserId\null, tags[], active, rev`One owner by construction. A table cannot shrink below its claims, or be deactivated while it holds claims, unless a relocate in the same commit empties it.
caGuests (aggregate root)`id, eventId, ref (YEP-0001), externalKey, name{first,last,display}, designation, company, industryId, email, mobile, lookup{surnames[], altSurnames[], m4}, classification: preferred\regular\employee\unlisted, tags[], rsvp, ownerUserId\null, arrival{state: expected\checked_in\cancelled, at, by, via: qr\desk\reconcile}, approval{state, by, at, note}, substituteFor/substitutedBy, groupId, seat{tableId, seatNo\null, status: reserved\confirmed, at, by}\null, statusTokens:[{hash, issuedAt}] (at most 5), flags:[{kind:'reverified_other_device', at}], notify{state, lastAt, attempts}, deskOnly, active, rev`The seat lives on the guest, so one guest cannot hold two seats. seat=null means no claim. A release writes history, not a third status. ownerUserId=null puts the guest in the Super Administrator's queue.
caChanges (append-only)`{seq, eventId, at, occurredAt?, actorId, via: staff\self\reconcile, type, opId, guestIds[], tableIds[], diff, reason}`Field-level diffs, about 200 B each, about 0.6 MB by the end of the event. The same log is the spec's approval and change history (s.7), the live-sync cursor, the idempotency store and the post-event report.
caOutbox`{id, eventId, guestId, kind: assignment\update\resend, to, subject, body, state: queued\sending\sent\failed, attempts, lastError, createdAt, sentAt}`The body is rendered from a seat snapshot when the message is queued. A failure never touches seat or arrival (spec s.6).

Derived, never stored: table counts (claimsAt), queue(owner) (owned, checked in, not seated), superQueue (no owner, unlisted pending, flagged, released-and-arrived), and the assignment status. Two lazy in-memory maps per event, guestsByLookupSurname and claimsByTable, are rebuilt whenever seq changes. Commit-time checks recount from the source, so a stale cache can never allow an overbooking.

A3. Invariants (one checkInvariants, used everywhere)
#InvariantHow it is enforced
I1claimsAt(t) <= t.capacity <= event.hardLimit (10), counting every claim whatever its statusChecked on every post-state
I2A guest holds at most one seatStructural (single seat field)
I3(tableId, seatNo) is unique among claimsChecked
I4Each guest and each table has at most one owner, and that owner is active staffStructural plus checked
I5Exactly one Super Administrator per eventStructural; changed only by setSuperAdmin
I6A substitute and the original invitee never both hold a seatThe seat moves inside one commit; checked
I7A guest is checked in only onceState-machine guard; a replay is idempotent
I8A table with claims cannot be deactivated or shrunk below its countChecked
I9A reserved seat never expiresNo timer touches seats; only release clears one
I10The actor writes only the guests and tables they own (the Super Administrator writes any)Re-read from the current state on every commit
A4. The unit of work
// server/customapp/events/commit.js — synchronous by design; a test greps this file for 'await'
export function commit(ctx, eventId, user, cmd) {
  const ev = mustEvent(ctx.db, eventId);
  if (ev.flags.readOnly && !cmd.reconcile) throw refusal('read_only');
  const role = roleOf(ev, user);                              // re-read every call: permission changes apply at once
  const prior = ctx.idem.get(ev.id, cmd.opId);                // Map rebuilt from caChanges at boot
  if (prior) return prior.sameBody(cmd) ? prior.result : refusal('op_replay_mismatch');
  const plan = ENGINE[cmd.type].plan(ctx.db, ev, role, cmd, ctx.now()); // pure; throws Refusal(code, fresh)
  expectRevs(plan, cmd.expect);                               // 409 stale, with fresh options
  checkInvariants(ctx.db, ev, plan);                          // I1-I10 on the post-state, via claimsAt()
  const undo = apply(ctx.db, ev, plan);                       // rev++, ev.seq++, push a compact caChanges entry
  try { ctx.db.save(); } catch { undo(); throw new HttpError(503, 'Not saved. Try again.'); }
  afterCommit(ctx, ev, plan);                                 // audit.record, outbox.enqueue: sync, no awaits
  return plan.result;                                         // {seq, revs, ...}
}

Every event write is registered through mutation(). At startup it refuses an AsyncFunction, and at run time it refuses a handler that returns a thenable (Craftsman N1). The process stays single: no queue mode, no cluster and no worker threads that write. The public selfCheckIn uses the same commit() with a synthetic actor (via:'self'), and its plan can only check a guest in.

Command set (one endpoint, POST /api/customapp/events/:id/commands {type, opId, expect, ...}):

  • checkIn and undoCheckIn. For a preferred guest, checkIn turns reserved into confirmed.

A released preferred guest goes to the Super Administrator's queue.

  • assign {placements[], allowSplit}. A group is one commit for all its members, and a split needs

allowSplit:true.

  • transfer and swap. The actor's scope must cover every guest and table involved; otherwise

only the Super Administrator can do it.

  • reserve and release. Only the Super Administrator, or the owner when releaseDelegable is

set. A reason is required, and nothing is ever released automatically.

  • createUnlisted, approve and reject. Staff create, and the Super Administrator decides.
  • substitute {originalId, newGuest}. The seat changes hands in one commit.
  • delegateGuests and delegateTables. Only the Super Administrator. Seats are never touched.
  • table.add, table.update, table.relocate, setFlag, notify and setSuperAdmin.

setSuperAdmin needs customapp.manage, password re-entry and a reason, and it is audited.

A5. Routes

Signed in (customapp.use; customapp.manage to create events or set the Super Administrator):

Method & pathPurpose
GET /api/customapp/appsthe APPS registry for the module landing page
GET/POST /api/customapp/events, GET/PATCH /api/customapp/events/:idevents the caller has a role in; settings, industries, staff and flags (the PATCH carries rev)
POST /api/customapp/events/:id/super-adminaudited transfer with re-authentication (the only event power a CORE admin has)
GET /api/customapp/events/:id/changes?since=<seq>&tab=<id>&wait=live sync (A9). wait is reserved and ignored for now
GET /api/customapp/events/:id/recommend?guestIds=ranked eligible tables, split into mine and othersReadOnly
GET /api/customapp/events/:id/guests?q=&class=&status=&table=scoped search for assisted check-in, including by referral code
GET /api/customapp/events/:id/referral/:codethe real failure reason and what the guest typed (masked)
POST /api/customapp/events/:id/commandsevery write, through commit()
POST .../import (preview, with collision report) then POST .../import/:jobId/applytwo-step CSV/XLSX import; the apply is one commit
GET .../export.json, .../export.csv?kind=, .../plan.pdfexport JSON v1, CSV and printable plan (Super Administrator only)
GET .../invariantscheckInvariants over the whole event, used by the 60 s self-check and the load script

Signed out (/api/public/event/:code/*, answered only when the event is live, never in the generic per-IP buckets):

Method & pathReturns
GET /api/public/event/:code`{name, date, state: closed\open, opensAt}` and nothing else
`POST /api/public/event/:code/verify {surname, last4 \email, k?}`one exact match → commit(selfCheckIn) (idempotent) → {token, state, firstName, table?, seat?}; anything else → the uniform referral reply (C1)
GET /api/public/event/:code/status with header X-Event-Token and ?since=`{seq, pollMs, state: pending\seated\see_desk, title, firstName, table, seat} or {seq, pollMs, unchanged:true}`

Pages: /event/<code> serves public/event.html. After verification, history.replaceState adds the token as a URL fragment (#s=<token>), which the browser never sends to the server, and the page also keeps a localStorage copy. The API reads the token only from the header, so it never appears in a path, a query string, a monitor key or a referrer.

A6. Error contract

Refusals come from one frozen catalogue in shared/customapp/refusals.js:

StatusCodes
409 (state conflict)stale, table_full, seat_taken, not_eligible, no_eligible_seat, already_checked_in, guest_moved, table_has_claims, op_replay_mismatch, read_only, registration_closed
403 (authority)out_of_scope, needs_super_admin
503not_saved (the save threw; the state is unchanged)

Every staff refusal carries {error, code, fresh}. fresh holds the current guest state, the winner where it can be shared, and re-ranked options, so the client re-renders without a second call. Optimistic UI is not allowed.

A7. Restore safety (the carve-out)

replaceData() always keeps the live caEvents, caTables, caGuests, caChanges and caOutbox when the live data holds at least one event. If the live server has no events (the standby case), it takes the backup's copy. This rule is deliberately not the existing next.x?.length ? next.x : db.data.x pattern, which prefers a non-empty backup and would bring back stale check-ins. It covers Admin "Restore a backup", "Erase all data" and demo data. While any event is live, the Admin restore screen says "Event data is not part of a CORE restore". The module switch in Admin panel → Modules refuses to turn CUSTOM APP off while an event window is open, unless the Super Administrator confirms (Skeptic N13).

A8. Storage decision gate

db.json is the one authoritative copy. There is no mirror, no dual-write and no capacity check in the browser. On 28 Nov, db.json size and save() p50 and p95 are measured on a copy of production:

save() p95Action
Under 50 msnothing to do
50 to 150 msin this order: compact JSON (db.js:125 pretty-prints today), coalesced 2 s outbox status writes, a resize to t3.small on 7 Dec (check for an Elastic IP first)
Over 150 ms on 30 Novsplit into data/customapp/<eventId>/ (about 2 dev-days, on the offsite.js documents/ precedent), and email drops to "display + verbal" to pay for it
A9. Live sync

changes?since=<seq> returns {seq, pollMs, unchanged:true} when nothing has changed. That is one integer comparison and a few bytes, with no board built. When seq has moved, it returns {seq, pollMs, board}, scoped by the access policy below and gzipped by Caddy. Change records use a closed set of type values from shared/customapp/, so an SSE transport added later can reuse them (push would carry only {seq}).

  • pollMs: staff boards 2 s, guest pages 4 s while pending and 10 s once seated, all with jitter.

Polling pauses on visibilitychange and backs off while offline. Under load it is raised to 8 s for guests and 4 s for staff, with no deploy.

  • Budget: about 30 staff devices at 2 s plus about 60 pending guest pages at 4 s is about 30

requests per second. An unchanged reply costs microseconds.

  • If the server's seq is behind since (a standby that was restored), the client receives a full

board.

Access policy:

ResourceSuper AdministratorStaff (owner)Staff (not owner)Guest
Event settings, staff, delegationread/writereadreadname and open state
Guest recordallown guestsname, company, industry and table only; no contact fieldsown title or first name, table and seat, via token
Table detailallown tables, read/writecounts, industries, free seats, read-only ("Not yours")none
Commandsallwithin scope (I10)noneselfCheckIn only
A10. Recommendation engine (shared/customapp/seating.js, pure)
eligible(table, guest, group) = table.active
  && classOK(table.classification, guest.classification, settings.mixedTables)
  && capacity(table) - claimsAt(table) >= group.length
  && !excluded(table.tags, group.tags)
classOK: preferred→{preferred,mixed}; regular→{regular,mixed}; employee→{employee,mixed}; unlisted→its approved class
tier 1: same industry already at the table · tier 2: related industry · tier 3: other eligible
within a tier: fits the whole group → more same-industry companions → fewer seats left after placement
               (fills gaps without forcing sequential filling) → table number (a deterministic tie-break)
result: [{tableId, tier, free, reasons[], selectable}] | {none:true, refer:'super_admin'}

commit() calls the same eligible() as a constraint, and the ranking stays advisory. reasons are human strings such as "Same industry: Banking, 3 of 6 seated", "Related: Insurance" and "4 seats left". When no table fits the whole group, the result includes split suggestions (the largest partitions that fit), and the client must send allowSplit:true. No prior art ranks tables by industry affinity at arrival (Scout), so the engine stays a deterministic tiered score with visible "why" chips.

A11. Email

afterCommit queues an assignment or update message when a seat is confirmed or changed and an address exists. outbox.js drains a capacity-1 FIFO (the n8n ConcurrencyQueue shape) outside commit():

  1. Mark the message sending.
  2. await sendMail(mailTransport(db)).
  3. Mark it sent or failed, and update guest.notify. Status writes are debounced to one save

every 2 s.

  1. Transient errors are retried three times with backoff. A manual Resend is a notify command.
  2. After a restart, a message left in sending for more than 2 minutes is queued again. That risks

a rare duplicate email rather than a lost one.

Messages use their own events From and Reply-To, so guest replies never land in the helpdesk IMAP mailbox as IT tickets. Names are encoded with composeMessage encoded words, and CR/LF is stripped on import. On 3 Dec there is a go/no-go on email. If it is "display + verbal", the outbox ships with emailPaused=true and the model does not change.

A12. Export JSON v1 (the one recovery artifact)

GET .../export.json returns {format:'customapp.event/1', exportedAt, eventId, seq, sha256, event, tables, guests (without token hashes), changes, outbox}. The same bytes feed:

  • the laptop pull;
  • the 5-minute copies on the server (data/backups/events/, keeping the last 24);
  • the printed pack (CSV and PDF are derived from it);
  • the standby top-up check;
  • the post-event archive handed to the client before the purge.

A v1.1 import will read this format and nothing else. Paper reconciliation is a series of commands sent with via:'reconcile', occurredAt and an opId through commit(), so a paper overbooking is refused and listed rather than applied.

User Experience (Advocate)

Three surfaces, each designed for a 390 px phone first.

U1. Guest page /event/<code>

The page is standalone HTML, under 40 KB, with no web fonts, a CSP hash, noindex and no-store.

StateWhat the guest sees
closed"Registration opens at 6:00 PM." There are no inputs.
verifyA Surname field (placeholder "e.g. Dela Cruz") and Last 4 digits of your mobile (inputmode="numeric", pattern="\d{4}", autocomplete="off"). A "Use email instead" link swaps the second field to the full email address.
seated"Welcome, Ms. Dela Cruz. Table 3, Seat 4". The table number is 56 px or larger.
pending"You're checked in. We're choosing your table. This page updates by itself." After 4 minutes it adds "Still choosing. A staff member can help at the desk".
referred"Please see the registration desk and show this code: R-482." Every failure, and the registrationOpen=false and readOnly states after doors open, look exactly like this.
offlineThe last known status, with "Will update when you reconnect".
  • A status line is honest about how old it is ("Updated 6:41 PM").
  • aria-live="polite" is set on the status region, and focus moves to the result heading.
  • Re-verifying from a second phone shows the same status, with no scolding and no second check-in.

Staff see a "verified again from another device" flag.

  • If email is cut, the "email sent" line is replaced by "Screenshot this screen".
  • The guest never sees "not found", "invalid", "already checked in", a countdown, a lock screen or

a 429.

U2. Staff "My queue"

This is the landing view for the Event staff account type, a view inside the SPA.

  • Sticky bar: event name, the staffer's name, and "Last updated 2 s ago". If updates stop for

15 s, it turns amber and reads "Reconnecting…".

  • Sections: Waiting for table (oldest first), Checked in and seated, and Find guest.
  • Card: name, company, an industry chip, a class badge in text, and a wait timer that turns

amber at 4 minutes and red at 8.

  • Seating in two taps. Tap the card, then tap a table. The first free seat is already selected,

and Confirm repeats the choice ("Seat at Table 5"). The bottom sheet shows up to 3 recommendations with their reason lines and a 10-dot meter. In the meter, filled means confirmed and a ring means reserved, and a text count sits beside it ("6 seated · 2 reserved · 2 free"). Below the recommendations are "Other eligible tables" (collapsed) and a read-only "Not yours" list. Seat chips 1 to 10 are mandatory for preferred guests, and taken seats are disabled.

  • Groups: select checkboxes, then "Seat together (n)". Only tables with n or more free seats

are ranked. Splitting needs an explicit "Split group" confirm. A group whose members have different owners shows "Send group to Super Administrator".

  • Recovery screens: a 409 re-renders the sheet in place with the guest still selected, for

example "Table 5 just filled. Updated options:". A guest the Super Administrator moved greys out with "Moved to Maria's queue by the Super Administrator, 6:52 PM" before it leaves the list. Nothing disappears silently.

  • Empty queue: "No one is waiting for you. Your tables: 5, 6, 7 (14 seats left)."
U3. Find guest / assisted check-in
  • Search by partial name, company or referral code. Entering R-482 fills in what the guest typed

and shows the real reason.

  • Contact details are masked ("•••• 4417").
  • Actions:
  • Check in (any staffer);
  • Seat (owner or Super Administrator only);
  • Clear cap and check in (one tap);
  • Add walk-in and Record substitute, which go to the Super Administrator's inbox.
  • A substitute is shown as one seat changing hands: "Seat 3-4: Juan Reyes replaces Pedro Reyes".
  • Check-in and seating are separate transitions with separate permissions.
U4. Event control (Super Administrator), in its simple form
  • Counts row: Invited · Checked in · Not yet arrived · Waiting for table · Needs approval ·

Seats left.

  • Occupancy grid: one tile per table with its number, class label, meter with a text count,

and owner. A full table is dimmed and labelled "Full".

  • Exceptions inbox (Must): walk-in and substitute approvals, "No seat in scope", released

reservations whose guest has arrived, capped surnames, and the failure-rate alarm.

  • Workload table: staffer · waiting · longest wait · last active. A per-guest or per-table

"Move to…" action. Bulk redistribution is a follow-up.

  • Health strip (Operator): seq, save p95, outbox depth, the age of the newest export, 429

count, and the result of the invariant self-check. A violation shows a banner.

  • Flags are toggles whose confirm dialogs name the effect, for example "Turn off self check-in?

Guests will be sent to the desk." Releasing a reservation needs a confirm that names the guest, table and seat. Removing a table that has guests is disabled, with a "Move guests…" action.

U5. Setup → Import check

The import preview lists each kind of problem as a count with a fix action beside it:

  • ambiguous pairs (same normalised surname and same last 4): add the full email, add a direct

mobile, or mark "desk only";

  • guests with no mobile and no email: mark "desk only";
  • possible duplicates;
  • unknown classification or industry;
  • tables set above capacity.

"Open registration" stays disabled, with the reason "3 issues to resolve", until the list is clear or the Super Administrator acknowledges it.

U6. Accessibility and copy floor
  • Inputs at least 16 px and touch targets at least 48 px.
  • WCAG AA contrast in both CORE themes.
  • Everything works by keyboard.
  • Badges and meter states carry text, never colour alone.
  • prefers-reduced-motion is honoured.
  • Guests are addressed by title and surname.
  • Copy is written in sentences, never codes, and staff copy names the person and the time.
  • Every guest-facing dead end points to a person.
U7. Failover UX
  • An easel card under the QR: "If this page doesn't open, please go to the registration desk."
  • A staff desk card with the standby URL and "If the app restarts you'll be signed out. Sign in

again and you'll land back on your queue". Sign-in returns the user to the route they were on.

  • The print pack holds an A–Z list, per-table sheets and per-staffer sheets. Every page is stamped

"as of HH:MM". After recovery, "Reconcile paper seating" replays each entry through the normal commands and shows any conflict as a recovery card.

  • Guides: a new CORE guide, docs/guides/custom-app-events.md, for the Event staff and Super

Administrator roles. Its sections are "Seat a guest", "When a table fills up", "Find a guest at the desk", "Event control (Super Administrator)" and "If the system is down". It doubles as the spec's operator guide, and the desk card is printed from it. A whats-new line and the screenshots ship with it.

Risk Assessment (Skeptic)

The seating logic no longer carries a risk that blocks the plan. What remains are CORE safeguards that were built for an office ERP, not for a ballroom behind one NAT address. Each one is closed in code or configuration and proven by a test or a drill. A promise in a runbook does not count.

R1. Threats and controls
Threat / failureControlProof
The venue NAT trips the per-IP limits (api-anon 120/min, public-write 20/h, auth 20/min, signin-fail), so check-in stops at about the 20th guest/api/public/event/* is exempt from the generic buckets. Its own limiter has a per-event global ceiling (for example 600/min), a per-surname bucket for failures and a per-token bucket for polls, with a high per-IP ceiling. Staff: raise signInFailuresPerAddress (a setting) and the auth bucket for the event window only, audited, and put staff devices on the 4G hotspot.In-process test with limits ON; load tests on 4 and 6 Dec from one IP show zero 429s to real guests and staff
Guest enumeration or someone learning a colleague's tableExact match on the normalised surname only, with no fuzzy match, no candidate list and no first-name fallback. Every failure (no match, ambiguous, capped, malformed, unlisted, window closed mid-request) returns a byte-identical body and status, padded to the success path's timing, plus a random R-xxx code from one pool.Body comparison test (minus the code); timing parity measured under load
Brute force on the last 4A soft cap of 8 distinct wrong last-4 values per (event, normalised surname) in 15 minutes. Retyping the same wrong value counts once. It clears after 15 minutes or with one tap at the desk. There is no global lockout: more than 30 failures in 5 minutes raises an alarm to the Super Administrator.Public test file pins the numbers
Remote pre-arrival spoofingA server-enforced activation window (window.opensAt). Before it opens, verify refuses and the page takes no input. Optional zero-typing QR key k (Should, D-table). The usher's "show this screen" step.Window boundary tests
A failed attempt forces a disk write (one whole-CORE JSON.stringify per request)Failed attempts, caps and referral codes live in bounded in-memory maps (TTL 3 h, at most 5,000 entries). Only a successful verify writes.Spy test: 1,000 failed verifies cause 0 db.save() calls
Status token leaks32 random bytes, stored as SHA-256 and looked up by hash. One token per device (at most 5), revocable by staff, and kept with the event so it survives a restart. Sent in the X-Event-Token header; the page keeps it in the URL fragment. Replies carry Cache-Control: no-store and Referrer-Policy: no-referrer, and only the title or first name, table, seat and state. Caddy has no log directive, and none may be added.A test that t= or the token never appears in monitor, audit or devlog output; curl -I on staging to confirm Caddy does not override Referrer-Policy
The CORE admin * acts as a second Super AdministratorEvent authority is event data: eventCan(user, event, action, target) checks superAdminUserId and the delegations. A CORE admin's only event power is the audited setSuperAdmin transfer (2-step, a reason, the old holder revoked in the same commit).A CORE admin's seat write gets 403; after a transfer there is exactly one Super Administrator
Cross-scope writes, or a stale permission after a reallocationeventCan runs on every guest and destination table in a plan, and any element out of scope rejects the whole plan. Delegations are re-read at commit time and never cached.Scope tests; reallocation journey
A race on the last seat, a double tap, or a Wi-Fi retrySynchronous commit(), per-guest and per-table rev, and opId idempotency. A mismatched replay is refused.Interleaved handle() tests, real-socket race, fuzz
A new seat status is not countedclaimsAt counts every status, and a closed-enum test fails when SEAT_STATUSES changes without a classificationExhaustiveness test
An Admin "Restore a backup" erases check-insThe A7 carve-out (always keep live), plus a boxed warning in RESTORE.md §1 that it is forbidden while an event is liveRestore test: live seq 120, backup at seq 50, event unchanged; tried during the 6 Dec drill
The process dies on a stray promise (Node 22 exits on an unhandled rejection; no handler exists today)An unhandledRejection handler logs, records to the monitor and keeps serving. An uncaughtException handler logs, then exits, and systemd restarts in 3 s.Child-process test; an injected rejection during load test 2
A push restarts production on the nightThe freeze guard in remote-deploy.sh plus the GitHub production reviewer gateA deliberate push after the freeze starts, which must not deploy
Injection through the import (email headers, CSV formulas)Strip CR/LF and control characters from names and companies. Prefix cells beginning with = + - @, tab or CR with ' on export. Import capped at about 2 MB and 2,000 rows, with a dry-run diff, Super Administrator only. Once the event is live, a re-import merges by externalKey and never touches status, seat or owner.Import tests
Content-type confusion on the public routeOnly application/json is accepted, the body is capped at about 1 KB, and submitted fields are never echoed. Surnames are 1 to 80 characters after normalisation, with zero-width and control characters stripped. last4 must match ^\d{4}$. The email must be the full address (at most 254 characters). Check order: limiter, then window, then match.Public tests
PII spreads into backups and laptopsAudit labels use the guest ref and a masked name, never the mobile or email. The admin export leaves out event PII while the event is live. The purge clock starts 10 Dec and covers ca*, the nightly copies, the event exports and the offsite lifecycle.Purge checklist (Guardian sets the date)
The module is switched off by mistake mid-eventAdmin → Modules refuses while a window is open unless the Super Administrator confirmsUnit test on the modules route
R2. Normalisation rules (shared by import and verify)
  1. Apply NFKD and strip diacritics.
  2. Lowercase.
  3. Drop spaces, hyphens, periods and apostrophes.
  4. Strip the suffixes Jr., Sr., II, III and IV.
  5. Match against surnames[] plus the stored altSurnames[] (for example "dela Cruz" and "Cruz").
  6. Compare the last 4 on the digits-only form of the stored mobile, so "+63 917 123 4567",

"09171234567" and "917-123-4567" all compare on "4567".

R3. Residual risks accepted
  • Pre-arrival spoofing by someone who knows a colleague's direct mobile. Bounded by the window,

the usher check and the re-verify flag.

  • A restart signs out every staff device. Bounded by the freeze, the crash handler, the event

sign-in allowance and a rehearsed re-login. Persisted sessions would mean changing the VAPT-hardened auth.js 12 days out, which is the bigger risk.

  • One Super Administrator is a single point of failure. This is a spec requirement. It is

bounded by a CORE-admin transfer rehearsed to take under 2 minutes.

  • The in-memory caps and referral codes reset on a restart. That fails toward the guest, and

staff can still search by name.

  • Up to 5 minutes of data on the box, and 15 off it, if the instance is lost. Closed by

replaying paper and the export through commands on the standby.

6 DecData freeze. Load test 2 with the kill -9 drill. Dress rehearsal with real staff accounts and 2-step sign-in. Last feature work.Fallback order applied to any open Sev-1
7 DecCode freeze at 18:00. GitHub production reviewer gate on. Resize if the gate asked for it.Event build tagged
8 DecDEPLOY_FREEZE from 12:00. Full backup. Offsite copies every 15 minutes. Print the pack.Event-ready
9 DecThe event, with the Super Administrator, a standby CORE admin for break-glass, the on-call developer and the warm standbyYEP 2026
10 Dec +Lift the freeze at 09:00. Archive the export. PII purge. Retro. v1.1 import reader. Generalise only if a second client app exists.Post-event report

Effort: about 14.5 to 15 dev-days in 9 build days, at CORE's observed cadence (MOD-08 went from engine to release in about 3 days). Craftsman's roughly 3 days of test work is inside this figure, written with each command. 6 to 8 Dec are buffer.

S3. Fallback order

Apply these one at a time, at the first missed tier date:

  1. Email becomes display plus verbal.
  2. The dashboard becomes counts plus the occupancy list.
  3. Groups become one-by-one assignment with a capacity hint.
  4. The seat picker for non-preferred guests is dropped. Seats stay mandatory for preferred guests.
  5. The presence key is dropped.
S4. Success metrics
  • Hard gates (the invariant scan after each load test and after the event): zero overbooked

tables, zero duplicate check-ins, zero double seats, zero holds, zero reservations released by expiry, and exactly one Super Administrator.

  • Flow: QR scan to checked in has a median under 30 s. Checked in to seated has a p90 under 2

minutes at peak.

  • What to invest in for event #2: the share of guests who self-verify versus those helped at the

desk, the share seated at a recommended table, and email delivered, failed and resent.

  • Operations:
  • at least 4 production deploys exercise the guard and the rollback before 7 Dec;
  • save() p95 under 100 ms at the load profile;
  • zero 429s to genuine guests;
  • staff back in, or on paper, within 3 minutes in the kill drill.

Prior Art and n8n Patterns (Scout)

P1. What we borrow and what we reject
ConcernSourceDecision for CORE
Guest verify answers at oncen8n Form Trigger responseMode: onReceived (nodes-base/nodes/Form/v2/FormTriggerV2.node.ts, common.descriptions.ts)Borrow. Verify returns at once with a token, and it never waits on staff.
Waiting page polls a status endpointn8n packages/cli/src/webhooks/waiting-forms.ts (WAITING_FORMS_EXECUTION_STATUS)Borrow. The guest status endpoint is polled at the server's pollMs.
Opaque resume token, uniform bad-token replyn8n waiting-webhooks.ts validateToken (length check, then timingSafeEqual); form auth token bound to node, webhook and executionBorrow the binding, not the JWT. A random 32-byte token, stored hashed and bound to (event, guest), with no signing key. A lookup by hash needs no constant-time compare.
Production URL live only while the workflow is activen8n live-webhooks.ts vs test-webhooks.tsBorrow. registrationOpen plus the activation window; outside it, every input gets the same neutral reply.
Bot filterForm Trigger ignoreBots (isbot)Borrow the idea, not the dependency. Reuse the helpdesk trap field and minimum fill time (server/helpdesk.js:454-542) and a short user-agent denylist.
Strict CSP on public pagesn8n getHtmlSandboxCSPCORE's hash-based pagePolicy already matches it. event.html gets the same treatment.
Capacity counter plus FIFO of waiting promisesn8n packages/cli/src/concurrency/concurrency-queue.tsBorrow for the email outbox only (capacity 1). Reject for seats: a seat request fails fast with a 409 and never waits in a queue.
Explicit reserve() and release()n8n concurrency-capacity-reservation.tsThe model to follow if holds are ever added (an explicit release, never two per guest). Not built now.
Push abstraction and SSE handshaken8n push/abstract.push.ts, push/sse.push.ts (a connectionKey replaces the older connection, 60 s ping, typed {type,data}, X-Accel-Buffering: no)Hold until after 9 Dec. If it is added, copy these semantics, and push carries only {seq}. The typed change records already prepare for it.
Queue mode (Bull/Redis), multi-main pub/subn8n push/index.ts shouldRelayViaPubSubReject. A second process breaks single-writer atomicity and brings a pub/sub relay with it.
Credentials kept server-siden8n credentials storeCORE already has server/secrets.js and the helpdesk SMTP. No new secret store.
Write contract: nonce, reason codes, force + datetime for late uploadspretix check-in APIBorrow opId, typed refusals with the current seq, and occurredAt (on reconcile only).
Capacity counts every blocking claim; lock before the availability checkpretix quotas and lockingBorrow as claimsAt() inside the synchronous commit().
Restrict which kiosk search fields existCvent OnArrivalBorrow. No fuzzy search in public; partial search only behind sign-in.
Offline devices that accept the same guest twiceFooEvents and Ticketsolve warningsAvoid. No device-side offline mode. Paper plus replay through commit().
P2. Reuse inventory already in CORE
  • public/js/vendor/qrcode.js (qrcode-generator 1.4.4) for the reception QR sheet.
  • shared/csv.js (parseCsv, formula-safe export).
  • server/pdf/office-core.js (readZip, parseXml) for an .xlsx reader of about 80 lines.
  • server/security.js pagePolicy.
  • server/audit.js (hash-chained) for spec s.13 events.
  • randomBytes(32).toString('base64url'), as already used at server/helpdesk.js:518.
P3. Technology radar for this event
RingItems
AdoptShort poll with pollMs and since=seq; a synchronous single-writer commit; typed 409s; opId
HoldLong-poll; SSE (until after 9 Dec, then a trial for the next CUSTOM APP); WebSocket, Redis/Bull, clustering; device-side offline or a PWA; seat holds with a TTL
RejectHMAC-signed URLs, IP allowlists for guests, fuzzy public search

Operations (Operator)

O1. 28 Nov ops half-day (code, about 0.5 dev-day, merged before any Tier 0 push)
# remote-deploy.sh — the first line that does real work, before the unit reinstall and update.sh
if [ -f "$APP/data/DEPLOY_FREEZE" ] && [ -z "${DEPLOY_FREEZE_OVERRIDE:-}" ]; then
  echo "Deploys are frozen ($(cat "$APP/data/DEPLOY_FREEZE")). Nothing was changed."; exit 3
fi
# update.sh — before `systemctl stop`
rm -rf /opt/ltdc-erp-prev && rsync -a --exclude /data/ "$APP/" /opt/ltdc-erp-prev/
# /usr/local/sbin/ltdc-erp-rollback: stop; rsync -a --delete --exclude /data/ /opt/ltdc-erp-prev/ $APP/; chown/chmod as update.sh; start; health loop
  • The workflow gets an optional freeze_override input, and the override is written to the

audit log or devlog. Exit code 3 must show as a failed deploy job, never a silent one.

  • The unhandledRejection logger and the uncaughtException log-then-exit, as in R1.
  • Measurements over SSM, read-only:
  • db.json size;
  • 50 runs of JSON.stringify + writeFileSync on a copy, giving p50 and p95;
  • free -m and the instance type;
  • aws ec2 describe-addresses (an Elastic IP is needed before any resize).
O2. Staging = restore drill = load target = warm standby (1–3 Dec, about 3 h operator time)
  • Build staging with RESTORE.md §2 from the latest offsite copy, on the same instance type as

production, with its own hostname and a certificate issued in advance.

  • Before the first start, set emailPaused=true and point the SMTP at a sink address.
  • Time each step. That is the measured RTO, and the target is under 30 minutes from "primary lost"

to "staff on the standby URL".

  • Keep it running until 10 Dec (about US$0.30 a day), and top it up from the newest offsite copy at

T-3h on 9 Dec. The standby is for staff only; there is no QR redirect for guests.

O3. Runtime levers (no deploy), each shown working on 5 Dec

registrationOpen, emailPaused, pollMs (returned in every poll reply) and readOnly (refuses every commit except reconcile commands from the Super Administrator, and still serves reads). The module kill switch already exists. Flag changes are Super Administrator only and audited.

O4. Recovery point

At most 5 minutes on the box (the export copy, keeping the last 24) and at most 15 minutes off it (offsite every 15 minutes during the window). On 8 Dec, after the last deploy, add a systemctl edit ltdc-erp-offsite.timer drop-in with OnCalendar=*:05/15. Deploys reinstall the unit but not drop-ins, and the freeze keeps it in place. It is removed on 10 Dec. The laptop pulls the export every print cycle. The health strip shows the age of each copy.

O5. Freeze
  • The GitHub production reviewer gate is on from 7 Dec 18:00.
  • touch /opt/ltdc-erp/data/DEPLOY_FREEZE at 8 Dec 12:00, removed 10 Dec 09:00.
  • CI still runs on every push during the freeze, because the guard sits after the test gate.
O6. Event-day runbook (in the CORE guide)
  • T-3h:
  • Confirm the freeze file and the gate are on.
  • Check the offsite drop-in with systemctl list-timers.
  • Run a manual offsite backup and confirm it in the Security monitor.
  • Top up the standby.
  • Smoke-test on the rehearsal guest: verify, check in, seat, status, email to the sink.
  • Confirm the newest export is under 5 minutes old and the laptop has pulled it.
  • Print the pack, every page stamped "as of HH:MM".
  • Check staff devices: signed in, on 4G, charged.
  • Doors: turn on registrationOpen. The on-call developer sits beside the Super Administrator

with ltdc-erp-rollback, the runbook and the standby URL.

  • Act on any of these:
  • 5xx on event routes above 1% for 2 minutes;
  • 2 failed external uptime checks in a row;
  • save() p95 above 250 ms;
  • the oldest pending guest waiting more than 3 minutes;
  • more than 5 email failures;
  • any red in the invariant self-check;
  • free disk under 1 GB.
  • Degrade ladder (each step is reversible, and none needs a deploy):
  • pollMs to 8 s for guests and 4 s for staff.
  • emailPaused.
  • registrationOpen=false, so check-in is staff-assisted only.
  • Paper by owner partition, with readOnly on.
  • Standby (staff URL only), then replay the paper sheets and the newest export as commands.

Never run RESTORE.md §1 while an event is live.

  • Close:
  • Turn off registrationOpen.
  • Take the final CSV and JSON export.
  • Run a manual offsite backup.
  • Set the retention date.
  • 10 Dec, 09:00:
  • Remove the freeze file and the timer drop-in.
  • Resize back.
  • Snapshot staging, then decommission it.
  • Start the PII purge clock.
O7. Load tests (scripts/event-load.mjs, standard library only, one source IP, limits ON)
RunProfilePass
1 (4 Dec)300 guest status pages at 4 s with jitter; 15–30 staff boards at 2 s; 40 verify-and-check-in in 10 minutes; a last-seat race on 3 contexts; one injected unhandled rejectionZero 429s to real guests and staff; save() p95 under 100 ms; event-loop delay p99 under 200 ms; invariants clean
2 (6 Dec, real list)Run 1, plus systemctl kill -s KILL ltdc-erp at minute 5, plus a CORE Admin restore attempted while the window is open, plus a Super Administrator transferStaff back in, or on paper, within 3 minutes; no committed check-in lost (compared with the export taken just before); the event is untouched by the restore; the transfer takes under 2 minutes; reconciliation through commands gives zero violations

Cost: under US$10 in total (the resize, staging and standby, offsite PUTs, and a free uptime check).

Quality Strategy (Craftsman)

Q1. Principles
  • The engine is a pure, synchronous plan(state, cmd, actor, now). The hardest guarantees can

therefore be tested in milliseconds without a database.

  • One checkInvariants() runs in four places: after every engine test, after every fuzz step,

at the end of the load script, and in the 60 s self-check in production. A checker test breaks each rule by hand, so the checker cannot be vacuous.

  • About 120 focused tests that run alone in under 5 s (npm run test:event). Each test is written

with its command, in the same commit.

  • Tests assert refusal codes, never message text. A catalogue test fails if any code has no

test that produces it.

  • After the 6 Dec freeze, every fix lands with a test that failed before it.
Q2. Test files by tier
FileKey casesTier
customapp-engineassign; table_full counting reserved seats; seat_taken; not_eligible; stale with fresh options; check-in once; preferred retrieval; release by an actor only; transfer keeps the old seat until the new one validates; swap atomic; group all or none; substitute moves the claim0
customapp-invariantseach rule broken by hand is caught; SEAT_STATUSES is closed; no file other than seating.js counts by seat.status0
customapp-fuzzseeded random sequences of assign, check-in, release, transfer, swap, substitute, group, reclassify, deactivate and delegate over reserved and confirmed guests, wrong scopes and stale revs; zero holds; no release by expiry; refusals only from the catalogue; prints the seed (200 seeds in CI, 1,000 at freeze)0
customapp-apimodule gating; mutation() refuses async functions and thenables; typed 409s; opId replay is idempotent and a mismatch is refused; one audit entry per write; when save throws, the state is unchanged, nothing is audited and the reply is 5030
customapp-restorerestoring an older backup, "Erase all data" and demo data all leave the live ca* and seq untouched; standby case: no live events, so the backup's events are taken0
customapp-flagsevery flag is checked inside commit() or in the poll reply; readOnly refuses every mutation with read_only and still serves reads0
customapp-exportexport v1 matches a golden fixture; contains every claim; no PII beyond the agreed fields; the 5-minute writer uses the same function0
customapp-importheader aliases; normalised surnames; last 4 derived; re-import idempotent; collision report finds every ambiguous pair and every guest with no contact; CR/LF stripped; formula-safe export0
customapp-processthe unhandledRejection logger records and does not exit (spawned child process)0
customapp-racereal sockets: 10 parallel requests for the last seat give one 200 and nine 409s; two parallel check-ins of one guest give one check-in; invariants [] (×5 at freeze)0
customapp-ranksame > related > other industry; full and ineligible tables never listed; deterministic tie-break; group fit; scope filter1
customapp-scopeEvent staff see only CUSTOM APP; a CORE admin is not the Super Administrator unless designated; one owner per guest and table; a permission change applies on the next request; a reallocation leaves confirmed claims intact1
customapp-public (limits ON, TRUST_PROXY=1)300 verifies plus polling from one x-forwarded-for give zero 429s while the soft cap still trips; zero saves on failure; byte-identical replies; window boundaries; cap of 8 distinct values in 15 minutes, a repeated value counts once, staff can clear it; alarm with no lockout; token of 32 bytes stored hashed, revocable, survives a restart; re-verify idempotent and flagged; no other guest's data in any reply; CSP hash present; the token never appears in logs1
customapp-syncchanges?since returns only newer changes plus pollMs, and ignores wait; a reallocated staffer's next poll drops the old queue1
customapp-outbox (fakeSmtp)email sent after the save; an SMTP failure leaves the assignment intact; resend is a new attempt; emailPaused queues without sending; no address means not applicable2
customapp-reusethe acceptance-critical engine cases over YEP and "Test Gala" (capacity 8, no employee tables, no seat numbers); events are isolated; grep finds no YEP string in server/customapp2
customapp-acceptancethe 12 named spec scenarios (Q3)per row

Browser: one "Event night, four devices" journey in scripts/journeys.mjs (a guest phone, two staff phones and the Super Administrator's desk), landing with Tier 1. It covers:

  • the last-seat contest, with the recovery screen checked by its visible text;
  • one journey per guest verification state;
  • the cap cleared at the desk in one action;
  • refusal after a reallocation;
  • "Import check" blocking "Open registration";
  • nothing wider than 390 px.

Q4. Gates
  • Per edit: npm run test:event in under 5 s, with fuzz at 50 seeds.
  • Per push: the full npm test plus npm run journeys, as today.
  • Per tier:
  • that tier's acceptance rows are green;
  • the version bump, devlog entry, guide, whats-new line and retaken screenshots land in the same

commit (test/guides.test.js);

  • customapp is added to the modules tests (modules-roadmap, favorite-modules).
  • Freeze candidate (6 Dec): fuzz at 1,000 seeds, the race test ×5, load test 2 green, and a

tagged commit.

  • Review checklist for every event PR:
  • no await in engine.js or commit.js;
  • every write goes through mutation();
  • no capacity count outside claimsAt();
  • refusals come from the catalogue;
  • any new rule is added to checkInvariants();
  • tests assert codes, not text;
  • no YEP string in server/customapp.

Tension Resolutions

TensionAgentsResolutionReasoning
Where event data lives: per-event state.json + log.jsonl vs shared db.jsonArchitect, Operator, Strategist, Scout, Skepticdb.json ca* collections, with the A7 carve-out. Split into data/customapp/<eventId>/ only if save() p95 is above 150 ms at the 30 Nov gate.The two crossed over in Round 2 and converged in Round 3. A split would change the offsite allowlist (offsite.js:265), the cron, the restore check and RESTORE.md, none of it drilled, 12 days out. The carve-out closes the restore hazard that drove the split, following an existing precedent, in one line plus a test.
How to close the "Admin restore erases check-ins" hole: refuse the restore while a window is open vs keep live event dataSkeptic, Architect, Craftsman, OperatorAlways keep the live ca* when the live data holds an event; take the backup's when it holds none (standby). A notice on the restore screen. RESTORE.md §1 is forbidden while live.Skeptic accepted either option, provided it is a code guard and tested. Keep-live leaves the CORE restore usable for unrelated reasons. Craftsman showed that the existing ?.length pattern would be wrong. Operator's standby edge case is covered by the "no live events" branch.
Event-only import / restore endpointOperator, Strategist, Skeptic, ArchitectWon't before 9 Dec. The export JSON v1 format is frozen now, with a golden test. Recovery means the standby plus replaying commands through commit().A recovery tool built in a hurry, used only under pressure and able to overwrite live data is the bigger risk. Skeptic's "rehearse it or it does not exist" is met by drilling the path that does exist (standby restore plus replay) on 6 Dec.
Seat holds (held)Scout, Craftsman, Strategist, Skeptic, AdvocateNo holds. seat.status ∈ {reserved, confirmed}, counted by a status-agnostic claimsAt(), with a closed-enum test.The spec marks holds optional. A single-request staff commit has no checkout window to protect, and a parked hold would block the last seat at peak. Counting claims rather than statuses removes the "forgot to count a status" bug class.
Live sync transport: long-poll vs SSE vs short pollArchitect, Scout, Operator, Strategist, CraftsmanShort poll on changes?since=seq, with pollMs from the server. The wait parameter is reserved. SSE on Hold until after 9 Dec.Removes the 120 s requestTimeout, Caddy buffering and monitor-exclusion work. It is the same contract that n8n's waiting-form uses. pollMs doubles as Operator's load lever.
Guest verification strictness: Skeptic's hard caps, lockouts and "do not re-show the seat" vs Advocate's no dead endsSkeptic, Advocate, Scout, CraftsmanLenient deterministic normalisation with exact match; uniform referral reply; soft cap of 8 distinct wrong values per surname in 15 minutes, cleared at the desk in one tap; an alarm with no global lockout; re-verify shows the seat and flags staff.The surname is not the secret; the last 4 and the activation window are. Every control fails toward the desk, never toward a lock screen. Advocate's distinct-value counting plus Skeptic's numbers satisfy both.
Presence key and desk codeStrategist, Skeptic, AdvocateThe zero-typing QR key k is a Should: built in Tier 1 (XS), enabled only after load test 1 passes, and the first item dropped in a slip. A typed desk code is Could, built only if the client asks.Skeptic and Advocate treat the window as the real control. Strategist values the key at XS. Gating it behind the load test keeps it off the critical path, and it costs guests nothing.
Status token transport: query ?t=, header, or path /s/<token>Architect, Scout, Advocate, Operator, SkepticPage: history.replaceState to the fragment #s=<token> plus a localStorage copy. API: the X-Event-Token header only.A fragment is never sent to the server, so the token cannot reach monitor keys (which keep the path), Caddy or referrers, and a reload still works. It meets Architect's "never in the path", Scout's header and Advocate's reload-survives.
Size of the ops layer (Operator's event-grade list vs Strategist's XS)Operator, StrategistAbout 1 dev-day of code (the 28 Nov half-day plus flags, export copy and health strip folded into tier items), plus CI-free operator time. Cut: event import, snapshot engine, persisted sessions, QR redirect.A restart at peak is the likeliest way the night fails, so the cheap guards come first. Everything at size M or on the critical path was deferred.
readOnly flag and the 60 s invariant self-checkOperator, Architect, StrategistBoth kept (XS each).One line in commit(), used by the Super Administrator during reconciliation. The self-check is the same checkInvariants() that the load test and fuzz suite use.
Kill-switch set: selfCheckIn (Skeptic, Craftsman) vs registrationOpen (Architect, Operator)Skeptic, Craftsman, Architect, OperatorFour flags: registrationOpen, emailPaused, readOnly, pollMs. selfCheckIn folds into registrationOpen. Once the window has opened, "closed" renders as the desk sentence (Advocate).Both flags had the same effect on guests (go to the desk). One fewer flag means one fewer state to test and explain. Status polls keep working when it is closed.
Persisted staff sessionsOperator, Skeptic, StrategistNot built. Mitigated by the freeze, the crash logger, an event-window sign-in allowance, staff on 4G, and a rehearsed re-login.Changing the VAPT-hardened auth.js 12 days out is the bigger risk. Skeptic's condition (no freeze without the crash handler and the sign-in allowance) is met.
Refusal code vocabularyArchitect, Advocate, Scout, CraftsmanOne frozen catalogue in shared/customapp/refusals.js (A6): 409 for state, 403 for authority, 503 for an unsaved change.Four lists overlapped. One shared catalogue, with a test per code, lets the browser render recovery screens and stops drift.
File layout: events/*.js + shared/seating.js vs engine.js + shared/customapp/*Architect, Craftsmanshared/customapp/{statuses,refusals,seating,invariants}.js and server/customapp/events/{model,engine,commit,routes,changes,public,outbox,io}.js.Keeps Architect's bounded context and Craftsman's pure engine file. claimsAt lives in one shared file, which a source test makes the only counter.
Freeze dates and where the guard sitsSkeptic, Operator, StrategistCode freeze 7 Dec 18:00 with the reviewer gate. DEPLOY_FREEZE from 8 Dec 12:00 to 10 Dec 09:00. The guard is the first line of remote-deploy.sh.This is the superset of the proposals. The guard has to run before the unit reinstall, so it goes in remote-deploy.sh, not update.sh.
uncaughtException: Node's default exit vs log and exitOperator, SkepticLog and record, then exit(1), and systemd restarts.The same behaviour as the default, but with evidence. State after an uncaught exception is undefined, so it does not keep serving.
save() thresholds: 50 ms vs 100 msCraftsman, Operator, Strategist50 ms is the 28 Nov static trigger for the levers. 100 ms p95 under the load profile is the pass mark. 250 ms is the event-night alarm. 150 ms on 30 Nov triggers the split.They measure different things, so all four are kept and each one is named.
Admin export of event PIIArchitect, Skeptic (Guardian absent)Left out while an event is live, unless "include events" is ticked (audited). Guardian sets the purge date.Otherwise the full-backup download is a second copy of the guest list on someone's laptop.
Email (Must in Round 1) vs date pressureStrategist, Advocate, OperatorBuilt as Tier 2 with a go/no-go on 3 Dec. emailPaused keeps the model intact either way.Spec s.6 allows a verbal or on-screen notice. The "email failure" scenario must work without email anyway.

Decision Log

DecisionOptions ConsideredChosenReasoning
Shape of CUSTOM APPOne-off YEP module; generic app builder; thin module + APPS registry + parameterised event engineThin customapp module with one events app, parameterised by eventIdEvent #2 reuses the engine as it is, for about 5% of the build. Abstraction is decided after 9 Dec, and only if a second client app exists.
Staff identitySeparate event logins; CORE accounts with event rolesCORE accounts; a new eventstaff account type (customapp.use); event roles and delegation are event dataReuses sign-in, 2-step, sessions and audit. The user chose this in the interview.
Super Administrator authorityCORE admin *; an event fieldevent.superAdminUserId (exactly one); a CORE admin can only transfer it, audited, with re-authenticationThe spec requires exactly one account, and * would create a second, invisible one.
StoragePer-event files; db.jsondb.json ca* collections, with a measured split gate on 30 NovUses the backup and restore path that is already drilled; one source of truth.
ConcurrencyLocks; Redis or queue mode; ETags only; a synchronous single-writer commitSynchronous commit() with no await, plus rev and opIdNode's single thread makes check-and-write atomic. Tested by a grep, the mutation() guard, a race test and fuzz.
Capacity modelStored counters; status-specific counts; claimsAt() over the guest seatclaimsAt() counts every claim; the seat is embedded in the guestA count that is never stored cannot drift, and one seat per guest is structural.
Seat statusesreserved, confirmed, heldreserved and confirmed only, as a closed enumThe spec requires reservations and makes holds optional.
Live syncSSE; WebSocket; long-poll; short pollShort poll with pollMs and since=seq; wait reservedNo new infrastructure; it is the lever under load and the n8n waiting-form precedent.
Guest identifierName only; a ticket QR per guest; surname + last 4 of mobile or the full emailSurname (normalised, exact) + last 4 digits, or the full emailChosen in the interview. The last 4 characters of an email carry no information, so the full address is used.
Public failure replySpecific errors; one uniform reply plus a referral codeByte-identical reply with a random R-xxx, timing padded, the reason kept for staffResists enumeration and never leaves a guest at a dead end.
Brute-force controlPer-IP limits; a global lockout; a per-surname soft cap8 distinct wrong values per surname in 15 minutes, cleared at the desk; an alarm only, globallyA per-IP limit breaks the venue NAT, and a global lockout lets one script stop everyone.
Pre-arrival spoofingDesk code; presence key; activation windowServer-enforced window; a presence key as a ShouldThe window is the real control and costs guests nothing.
Status tokenRe-derive from the identifiers; a JWT; a random hashed token32 random bytes, stored as SHA-256, per device (at most 5), revocable; header on the API, fragment on the pageThe n8n binding idea without a signing key; it never reaches logs.
Writes from public trafficPersist attempts; keep them in memoryOnly a successful verify writes; caps and referral codes live in bounded mapsOtherwise an attacker drives a whole-CORE JSON.stringify per request.
Rate limitingReuse api-anon/public-write; event bucketsEvent buckets per event, surname and token; staff sign-in allowance for the windowThe current limits stop check-in at about the 20th guest behind one IP.
Restore safetyA runbook line; refuse the restore; keep live ca*Keep live ca* (with the standby branch), a notice, and a RESTORE.md §1 warningA code guard with a test, which still leaves CORE restores usable.
IdempotencyNone; requestId; opIdopId on every write, stored in caChanges, a map rebuilt at boot, mismatch refusedCovers Wi-Fi retries, double taps and paper re-entry (pretix nonce).
Refusal transportMessage strings; typed codesHttpError(status, msg, {code, fresh}) plus a frozen catalogueThe client re-renders recovery screens without a second call; tests assert codes.
Recommendation engineSequential fill; ML; a deterministic tiered scoreSame > related > other industry; group fit, then gap fill, then a deterministic tie-break; "why" chipsThere is no prior art to copy, so explainable and testable wins. eligible() is shared with commit().

Change historyFull before/after images; field diffsA compact caChanges with field diffs (about 0.6 MB per event)Keeps db.json growth and save cost small. One log serves history, sync, idempotency and the report.

Offline contingencyA device-side PWA; pre-partitioned ranges; paper by owner plus replayPaper by owner partition, stamped "as of"; replayed through commit() with via:'reconcile'Table ownership already partitions the seats. Replay keeps every invariant.

TestingA large suite with coverage targets; focused tests plus an invariant oracleAbout 120 tests in under 5 s; one checkInvariants; 12 named acceptance tests; fuzz; a race test; a limits-ON single-IP test; 2 load tests and a chaos drillProves the guarantees cheaply; the acceptance tests are the client's evidence pack.

Rejected: queue mode, multi-main, push for nowCORE has no dependencies, and anything that adds a second process breaks atomicity.
